plan.md
# Plan: Inspect-style `report.html` for a poe task

## Objective

Turn what poe saves for a task in `poe/<task-slug>/` (plan, status, phase briefs, progress notes, summary, and the copied Claude Code transcripts in `logs/`) into one self-contained `poe/<task-slug>/report.html`. The page, styled after the Inspect AI log viewer, lets a person follow, step by step, what the orchestrator and each worker did: prompts, replies, tool calls and their results, errors, and token and cost totals.

The page has two later uses, which shape it now but are not built here:

- **Stacktrace:** when a run goes wrong, errored and denied tool calls can be found quickly and traced back to the step that caused them.
- **LLM-as-judge input:** all content is plain, server-rendered HTML text (no data hidden in JavaScript), so a model can be given the file as is.

## Scope

In scope:

- `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py` (new): a Python 3 script using only the standard library. `python3 poe_report.py poe/<task-slug>` reads that task directory and writes `poe/<task-slug>/report.html`.
- `tests/test_poe_report.py` and `tests/fixtures/` (new): `unittest` tests against a small, hand-written transcript fixture.
- `skills/poe/SKILL.md`:
  - a new `report [plan-path]` mode that (re)builds the report;
  - an automatic report build at the end of `execute` and `resume`, right after the log copy;
  - `report.html` added to the Files layout.
- `README.md`: the `report` mode, `report.html` in the layout, how to run the script by hand, and gitignore advice.

Out of scope:

- The LLM-as-judge itself, scoring, and any JSON or other machine-readable export.
- Converting to Inspect AI's `.eval` log format, or depending on `inspect-ai` or any other pip package. "Inspect style" means the look and layout, not the tool.
- A live server, auto-refresh, search boxes, charts, or comparing several tasks.
- Changing how logs are copied. The report works with whatever is in `logs/`.
- Rendering Markdown to HTML. The task's `.md` files are shown as preformatted text.
- The older draft at `plans/poe-report-dashboard/plan.md` (written before output moved to `poe/`). This file replaces it; it is left untouched for the user to delete.

## Assumptions

- The layout is the one in the current `SKILL.md`: `poe/<slug>/{plan.md, status.md, summary.md, phases/*.md, progress/*.md, logs/<session-id>/<session-id>.jsonl, logs/<session-id>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl, agent-<id>.meta.json}`. Any of these may be missing, and there may be several sessions (one per `plan`, `split`, `execute`, `resume` run).
- The Claude Code transcript format is not documented and changes between versions. Checked on this machine (Claude Code 2.1.326) against existing transcripts:
  - Each line is a JSON record with a `type`. The records that matter are `user` and `assistant` (with `message.content` blocks `text`, `thinking`, `tool_use`, `tool_result`), `system`, and `cost-state` (`totalCostUSD`, `totalDuration`, `modelUsage`). Other types (`attachment`, `api-request`, `api-request-blob`, `mode`, `session-owner`, `atis-latch`, `last-prompt`, `ai-title`, `permission-mode`, `tools-used`, `file-history-*`, `queue-operation`, ...) are internal and skipped.
  - `assistant` records carry `timestamp`, `message.model`, and `message.usage` (input, output, cache read and cache creation tokens).
  - `user` records holding a `tool_result` block may carry `toolUseResult` and `permissionDecision` (`decision`, `source`). The block itself may have `is_error: true`.
  - A worker's `agent-<id>.meta.json` holds `agentType`, `description` and `toolUseId`. `toolUseId` matches the `id` of the `Agent` `tool_use` block in the main transcript, which is how a worker is linked to the step that started it.
- A tool result counts as an error when `is_error` is true, or as denied when `permissionDecision.decision` is present and not `accept`. Both are read defensively.
- Records are shown in file order (the order Claude Code appended them). The `parentUuid` tree is not reconstructed.
- `python3` (3.9 or later) is available wherever poe runs. This machine has 3.12.
- Skills substitute `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}`, so `SKILL.md` can call the bundled script by its path. **To be confirmed during implementation** against the skills documentation; otherwise use `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`.

## Approach

### 1. The script, `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`

One file of small functions, in three steps.

**Load.** Read the task's `.md` files as text. For each `logs/<session-id>/` folder, parse the main `.jsonl` and each `subagents/agent-*.jsonl` with its `.meta.json`. A line that is not valid JSON, or a record without the expected fields, is counted and skipped; it never stops the script.

**Build a simple timeline per transcript.** Turn the useful records into a flat list of steps, each with: time, actor (orchestrator or worker name), kind, and text. The kinds are:

- user prompt
- assistant text
- thinking (collapsed)
- tool call (tool name and its input)
- tool result (output; marked as an error or denial as above)
- system or hook message

Each tool call is paired with its result by `tool_use_id`. An `Agent` tool call links to the worker's own timeline. Per transcript, and in total, the script adds up tokens, the number of tool calls and errors, the model or models, the duration (first to last timestamp), and the cost from the last `cost-state` record when present.

**Render.** Write one HTML file with inline CSS and no external resources, so it opens offline and can be attached or emailed as a single file. The layout follows Inspect's viewer:

- **Sidebar:** anchor links to the overview, the errors list, the task documents, and each session, with its workers indented under it.
- **Overview cards:** the task slug, the date generated, the status table from `status.md` as written, the number of sessions and workers, tool calls, errors, tokens, cost, duration, and the number of skipped records.
- **Errors:** every errored or denied tool call across all transcripts, each with its tool name, a one-line excerpt, and a link to the step. This is the stacktrace view.
- **Task documents:** `plan.md`, `status.md`, `summary.md`, phase briefs and progress notes, each in a collapsible `<details>` block of preformatted text.
- **Session timelines:** one section per session, ordered by first timestamp, then one per worker. Each step is a row with a coloured left border by kind, a timestamp, and its content. Tool inputs and outputs are in monospace inside `<details>`, with the first few lines visible. Error rows are red. A worker section starts with its type, description, model and totals, and links back to the step that started it.
- Light and dark colours through `prefers-color-scheme`. `<details>` handles collapsing, so the page needs no JavaScript.

All text from the logs goes through `html.escape`, because transcripts contain arbitrary file contents and command output. Content is never truncated in the HTML. Long blocks are collapsed, not cut, so the file stays complete for a stacktrace or a judge.

If `logs/` is missing or empty, the report still renders the documents and says that no transcripts were found.

The script prints the path of the written report and exits 0. It exits non-zero only if the task directory does not exist or the report cannot be written.

### 2. Skill changes, `skills/poe/SKILL.md`

- Operating modes gain `report [plan-path]`: copy the logs (as in the Logs section), then run `python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/poe_report.py" poe/<slug>` and give the user the path of the report.
- Execute mode step 9 and `resume`: after writing `summary.md` and copying the logs, build the report the same way. A failure is reported in the reply but never blocks the task, the same rule as for log copies.
- Files layout: add `report.html   step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/`.

### 3. README

Add the `report` mode to the usage list, `report.html` to the layout, the manual command `python3 <plugin>/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py poe/<slug>`, and a note that `report.html` contains the full transcripts, so it belongs in `.gitignore` alongside `logs/`.

## Acceptance criteria

- `python3 -m unittest discover tests` passes. The tests cover:
  - parsing the fixture: the expected number of steps, tool calls paired with their results, an error flagged, and a worker linked by `toolUseId`;
  - skipping unknown record types and malformed lines without crashing;
  - HTML escaping: a `<script>` string in a tool result appears escaped in the output;
  - a task directory without `logs/` still produces a report.
- Running the script on real transcripts (a poe session from this project, including one with workers, copied into a scratch `poe/<slug>/logs/`) writes a `report.html` that opens in a browser and shows:
  - all sessions and their workers;
  - every tool call, in order, with its result;
  - every `is_error` tool result, listed in the Errors section;
  - token totals that match a separate sum over `message.usage` in the same files.
- The report opens with no network access (no external URLs in the file).
- `SKILL.md` documents the `report` mode and the automatic build. `README.md` matches it. `claude plugin validate .` still passes.
- `/poe:poe report <slug>` run with `claude --plugin-dir .` in a scratch directory produces `poe/<slug>/report.html`.

## Risks

- **Transcript format changes.** The parser relies on undocumented fields. Mitigation: read fields defensively, skip what is unknown, and show the count of skipped records in the overview, so silent loss is visible.
- **Missing logs.** A log copy can fail (for example, refused under a restrictive permission mode). Mitigation: the report degrades to documents only and says why.
- **Permission prompts.** Running `python3` on a script outside the working directory may need approval in restrictive modes. Mitigation: the same "report and carry on" rule as for logs. The README gives the manual command.
- **Sensitive data.** The report contains everything in the transcripts, including file contents and command output. Mitigation: gitignore advice. Nothing is uploaded.
- **Large files.** A long execute run can produce several MB of transcripts, and so a large HTML file. Acceptable for a local file. Collapsed `<details>` keep it usable in a browser.

## Testing and validation

1. Unit tests: `python3 -m unittest discover tests`, using a small, hand-written fixture in `tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/` with a main transcript, one worker transcript and meta file, one errored tool call, one unknown record type, and one malformed line. The fixture holds no real session data.
2. Real data: copy the transcripts of a real poe session from `~/.claude/projects/` into a scratch `poe/<slug>/logs/` (outside the repository, never committed), run the script, and check the report in a browser against the acceptance criteria above. Check the token totals with a short independent `python3` sum over the same files.
3. End to end: `claude --plugin-dir .` in a scratch directory, run `/poe:poe report <slug>` on that scratch task, and confirm that `report.html` is written and the skill reports its path.
4. Static checks: `grep -nE '(src|href)="https?:' report.html` finds no external resources (escaped URLs inside log text don't match), and `claude plugin validate .` passes.

# Summary: poe-report-dashboard

Execute run, 2026-11-17. All four units (01a, 01b, 02a, 03a) verified. Nothing committed.

## What was built

- `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`: Python 3, standard library only. `python3 poe_report.py poe/<slug>` writes `poe/<slug>/report.html` and prints its path; exits 1 if the task dir is missing or the file can't be written.
  - Single self-contained HTML file: inline CSS, no JavaScript, no external URLs, light/dark.
  - Sidebar, overview cards (including `status.md`), Errors list linking to each errored or denied step, task documents, then each session's timeline followed by its workers. `Agent` calls and worker sections link both ways.
  - All log text is escaped. Long blocks are collapsed in `<details>`, never cut.
  - Token usage is counted once per `message.id` (Claude Code repeats it on every record of one reply).
- `tests/test_poe_report.py` (29 tests) and the hand-written fixture `tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/`.
- `skills/poe/SKILL.md`: `report [plan-path]` mode, report build at the end of `execute`/`resume`, a `## Report` section with `python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/poe_report.py" poe/<slug>`, `report.html` in Files layout, `report` in argument-hint and in the Logs mode list.
- `README.md`: matching usage, layout, manual command, and the advice to gitignore `poe/*/report.html` with `poe/*/logs/`.

## What passed (observed)

- `python3 -m unittest discover tests`: 29 tests OK.
- `claude plugin validate .`: passed with warnings that were already there (no `version`, no icon).
- Real data (3 sessions, 3 workers, 2.5 MB of logs, in the scratchpad): an independent checker that does not import the script found 68/68 tool calls in order, each with its result; 9/9 `is_error` results in the Errors section; token totals equal (input 126, output 41,974, cache read 2,544,813, cache creation 208,354); no external refs, no `<script>`; 307 internal links all resolve. Report size 354 KB.
- End to end: `claude -p --plugin-dir <repo> --permission-mode acceptEdits --allowedTools "Bash(python3:*) Bash(mkdir:*) Bash(cp:*) Bash(find:*)" -- "/poe:poe report poe/real-check"` wrote `report.html` and the reply gave its path. Same checker: PASS.

## What remains uncertain

- **Not opened in a browser.** No report has been looked at visually.
- **Tokens and cost can disagree.** Tokens are summed from the transcripts; cost comes from Claude Code's `cost-state`, which also counts API calls with no transcript record (e.g. Haiku background calls). They matched for one session but not for another. A one-line README note would explain this; not added because it is beyond the plan.
- **Duration** uses step timestamps only, so a session with no assistant records shows 0s.
- **Headless log copy is denied** under the approved permissions (brace variables in the command, and `find` outside the working directory). Pre-existing behaviour, out of scope; the report still builds from whatever is in `logs/`.
- The approved headless command needed `--` before the prompt; the phase brief's version without it fails to parse.

## Blockers

None.

## Files

- Task: `poe/poe-report-dashboard/` (`plan.md`, `status.md`, `phases/`, `progress/01a-parser.md`, `01b-docs.md`, `02a-render.md`, `03a-validation.md`, `report.html`, `logs/`).
- Code: `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`, `tests/test_poe_report.py`, `tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/`, `skills/poe/SKILL.md`, `README.md`.
- Validation artifacts (scratch, not in the repo): `/private/tmp/claude-1128299617/-Users-sbandyopadhyay-projects-SCRIPTS-custom-claude-plugins-poe/e1650fd1-5110-41f6-944b-7f0774e47f42/scratchpad/03a/`.

# Phase 01: transcript parser, and skill/README docs

Depends on: approved split. Units 01a and 01b touch different files and run in parallel.

Plan: `poe/poe-report-dashboard/plan.md` (read Assumptions, Approach §1–3, Acceptance criteria).

## Unit 01a: load and timeline in `poe_report.py`, fixture, parsing tests

**Goal.** Create `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py` with the **Load** and **Build a simple timeline** parts of plan §1 (no HTML yet), the hand-written fixture, and the parsing tests. Phase 02 adds rendering to the same file on top of the interface below.

**Inputs to read.**

- `poe/poe-report-dashboard/plan.md`
- Sample real transcripts, to confirm field names (read with `head`/`python3`, never whole files; lines can be very long):
  - with workers: `~/.claude/projects/-private-tmp-claude-1128299617--Users-sbandyopadhyay-projects-SCRIPTS-custom-claude-plugins-poe-cb353a49-6d4d-41b6-911e-14acc86a0355-scratchpad-02a-e2e/7234187e-d86b-4afa-adca-ee152aac72bf.jsonl` and its `7234187e-.../subagents/`
  - `poe/poe-report-dashboard/logs/*/*.jsonl`

**Files it may change.** Create only:

- `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`
- `tests/test_poe_report.py`
- `tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/**`

**Interface for phase 02** (plain dataclasses, standard library only; names may be adjusted if recorded in the progress notes):

- `Step`: `index`, `timestamp` (str or None), `kind` (one of `user`, `assistant`, `thinking`, `tool_call`, `tool_result`, `system`), `text`, `tool_name`, `tool_use_id`, `is_error`, `is_denied`, `anchor` (unique HTML id string), `result` (the paired `tool_result` Step for a `tool_call`, else None), `worker` (the linked worker Transcript for an `Agent` call, else None).
- `Transcript`: `name` (session id or `agent-<id>`), `actor` (`orchestrator` or the worker's description/type), `agent_type`, `description`, `steps`, `models` (set), token totals (`input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens`, `cache_creation_tokens`), `tool_calls`, `errors`, `first_timestamp`, `last_timestamp`, `cost_usd` (or None), `skipped` (count), `parent_step` (the `Agent` call Step, for workers).
- `Session`: `session_id`, `main` (Transcript), `workers` (list of Transcript).
- `Task`: `slug`, `task_dir`, `documents` (ordered list of `(relative_path, text)` for `plan.md`, `status.md`, `summary.md`, `phases/*.md`, `progress/*.md`), `sessions` (ordered by first timestamp), `skipped` (total).
- `load_task(task_dir: Path) -> Task`.

**Rules from the plan.** Skip unknown record types and malformed lines, counting them. Pair calls and results by `tool_use_id`. Link workers via `.meta.json` `toolUseId` regardless of the tool's name (`Agent` or older `Task`). Errors: `is_error` true; denied: `permissionDecision.decision` present and not `accept`. Keep file order. Never truncate text.

**Check before trusting usage.** Claude Code may write one `assistant` record per content block, each repeating the same `message.id` and `message.usage`. Check this in the real transcripts. If it happens, count usage once per `message.id`, and record the rule in the progress notes (phase 03's independent sum must use the same rule).

**Fixture** (hand-written, no real session data): `tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/` with `plan.md`, `status.md`, one `phases/01-x.md`, one `progress/01a-x.md`, and `logs/<fake-session-id>/` holding a main transcript and `subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl` + `.meta.json`. Include: one `Agent` tool call linked to the worker, one tool result with `is_error: true`, one denied result, one tool result containing `<script>alert(1)</script>` (used by phase 02's escaping test), one unknown record type, one malformed line, and a `cost-state` record.

**Tests** (`unittest`): expected step count; each tool call paired with its result; error and denial flagged; worker linked via `toolUseId`; unknown type and malformed line skipped and counted; token totals; a task dir without `logs/` loads with no sessions.

**Non-goals.** No HTML rendering, no `main()` writing files, no SKILL.md/README edits, no pip packages.

**Verification.**

```sh
cd /Users/sbandyopadhyay/projects/SCRIPTS/custom_claude_plugins/poe
python3 -m unittest discover tests -v
python3 -c 'import sys; sys.path.insert(0,"skills/poe/scripts"); import poe_report as p; from pathlib import Path; t=p.load_task(Path("poe/poe-report-dashboard")); print([(s.session_id, len(s.main.steps), len(s.workers)) for s in t.sessions], t.skipped)'
```

**Completion criteria.** Tests pass (observed). `load_task` runs without error on `poe/poe-report-dashboard` and on a scratch copy of the 7234187e session with workers (copy into the scratchpad, not the repo), with workers linked. Interface and the usage-dedup finding recorded in the notes.

**Progress notes:** `poe/poe-report-dashboard/progress/01a-parser.md`

phases/02-render.md
# Phase 02: HTML rendering and command-line entry

Depends on: unit 01a verified.

## Unit 02a: render `report.html`

**Goal.** Add the **Render** part of plan §1 to `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`, plus `main()`, so `python3 poe_report.py poe/<task-slug>` writes `poe/<task-slug>/report.html` and prints its path.

**Inputs to read.**

- `poe/poe-report-dashboard/plan.md` (Approach §1 "Render", Acceptance criteria)
- `poe/poe-report-dashboard/progress/01a-parser.md` (the interface actually built, and the usage rule)
- `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`, `tests/test_poe_report.py`, `tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/`

**Files it may change.** `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py` (add rendering and `main`; change the loader only to fix a bug, noted in the progress notes), `tests/test_poe_report.py` (add tests). Not the fixture, unless a test needs data it lacks (note why).

**What to build** (all from the plan):

- One HTML string, inline CSS, no JavaScript, no external URLs. Light/dark via `prefers-color-scheme`.
- Sidebar of anchor links: overview, errors, documents, each session with workers indented.
- Overview cards: slug, date generated, `status.md` as written (preformatted), sessions, workers, tool calls, errors, tokens, cost, duration, skipped records.
- Errors section: every errored or denied tool result with tool name, one-line excerpt, link to the step anchor.
- Documents: each in a `<details>` with `<pre>`.
- Session timelines, ordered by first timestamp, then each worker: one row per step with a coloured left border by kind, timestamp, content. Tool input/output in monospace `<details>` with the first few lines visible in the summary. Error rows red. Thinking collapsed. Worker sections start with type, description, model(s), totals, and a link back to the starting step; the `Agent` call links forward to the worker section.
- Every piece of log or document text through `html.escape`. Never truncate; collapse instead.
- No `logs/` or no transcripts: render documents and say no transcripts were found.
- `main`: exit 0 and print the path on success; non-zero with a message if the task dir does not exist or the file cannot be written.

Keep it plain: small functions, f-strings or simple string building, no template engine.

**Tests to add.** `<script>alert(1)</script>` from the fixture appears only escaped; no `src="http` / `href="http` in the output; a dir without `logs/` still writes a report containing the "no transcripts" message; `main` on a missing dir exits non-zero; error rows link to existing anchors.

**Non-goals.** No JavaScript, search, charts, JSON export, Markdown rendering, SKILL.md/README edits.

**Verification.**

```sh
cd /Users/sbandyopadhyay/projects/SCRIPTS/custom_claude_plugins/poe
python3 -m unittest discover tests -v
python3 skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task   # writes into the fixture dir: delete that report.html afterwards, do not commit it
grep -nE '(src|href)="https?:' tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/report.html; echo "external refs: $?"
```

**Completion criteria.** Tests pass (observed). The fixture report contains every section above (checked by grep or by the tests). No leftover generated `report.html` in the fixture dir.

## Unit 03a: real transcripts, independent token check, `/poe:poe report` end to end

**Steps.**

1. Build a scratch task `poe/real-check/` in a fresh scratch dir: copy a `plan.md` and `status.md` from any `plans/*/` dir, and copy into `logs/` the transcripts of session `7234187e-d86b-4afa-adca-ee152aac72bf` (with its `subagents/`, from `~/.claude/projects/-private-tmp-claude-1128299617--Users-sbandyopadhyay-projects-SCRIPTS-custom-claude-plugins-poe-cb353a49-6d4d-41b6-911e-14acc86a0355-scratchpad-02a-e2e/`) and one or two sessions from `~/.claude/projects/-Users-sbandyopadhyay-projects-SCRIPTS-custom-claude-plugins-poe/`.
2. Run `python3 <repo>/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py poe/real-check`. Check, by grep/python on the HTML and by comparing with the JSONL:
   - every session and worker has a section;
   - the number of tool-call rows equals the number of `tool_use` blocks in the files, in order, each with a result;
   - every `is_error: true` result is in the Errors section;
   - token totals equal an independent `python3` sum over `message.usage` written by this unit (same dedup rule as 01a);
   - `grep -nE '(src|href)="https?:' report.html` finds nothing.
   Note the HTML size; open in a browser only if a tool for it is available, otherwise say it was not visually checked.
3. End to end: in a scratch dir holding a copy of `poe/real-check/` (without `report.html`), run headless against the local plugin, e.g.
   ```sh
   claude -p --plugin-dir /Users/sbandyopadhyay/projects/SCRIPTS/custom_claude_plugins/poe \
     --permission-mode acceptEdits --allowedTools "Bash(python3:*) Bash(mkdir:*) Bash(cp:*) Bash(find:*)" \
     "/poe:poe report poe/real-check"
   ```
   using the permission mode recorded as approved in `status.md`. Confirm `report.html` is written and the reply gives its path. Record the exact command and whether any step was denied.
4. `claude plugin validate .` in the repo.
5. If something fails, record exactly what and why. Do not edit repository files; report back so the orchestrator can scope a fix.

**Non-goals.** No repository edits, no commits, no cleanup of `~/.claude/projects`, nothing on remote systems.

**Completion criteria.** Each check in steps 2–4 has an observed pass/fail with trimmed command output in the notes. Unverified items marked as such.

- `tests/test_poe_report.py` (new)
- `tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/` (new): `plan.md`, `status.md`, `phases/01-x.md`, `progress/01a-x.md`, `logs/11111111-2222-3333-4444-555555555555/11111111-2222-3333-4444-555555555555.jsonl`, `.../subagents/agent-a0000000000000001.jsonl` + `.meta.json`. All fake data. Main transcript contains: Agent call `toolu_agent_1` linked to the worker; `is_error` result (`toolu_error_1`); denied result (`toolu_denied_1`, decision `reject`, also `is_error` as in real data); `<script>alert(1)</script>` result (`toolu_read_1`, for phase 02's escaping test); unknown type `mystery-record`; one malformed line (line 14); a `cost-state` record (0.1234); `msg_main_1` split over two records (dedup test); ignored internal types `session-owner` and (worker) `attachment`.

## Tests run and observed results
- `python3 -m unittest discover tests -v`: Ran 15 tests, OK.
- Brief's one-liner on `poe/poe-report-dashboard`: `[('b046f754-...', 23, 0), ('5d0a8152-...', 19, 0)] 0` (2 sessions, no workers, 0 skipped).
- Scratch 7234187e task: 1 session, main 36 steps, 1 worker (`agent-a1c06f9ad1f6bb6e9`, actor "Execute unit 01-a hello.txt") linked to the `Agent` call `toolu_01WorK1HYL13BQvuibYY95jR` both ways; every tool call paired (0 unpaired); 3 denied Bash results flagged (is_error and is_denied); 1 worker error; skipped 0; main cost_usd 0.460544.
- Deduped session token sum (main + worker) = input 28, output 5876, cache read 334255, cache creation 42743, which matches the main transcript's `cost-state.modelUsage` exactly.

## Decisions and assumptions
- **Usage dedup (confirmed):** Claude Code writes one `assistant` record per content block; all records of one API message repeat the same `message.id` and an identical `message.usage` (7234187e main: 21 records / 9 ids, 0 ids with differing usage; worker 6/5; repo logs 10/5 and 12/6). **Rule: count usage once per `message.id` within a transcript; records without an id are each counted.** Phase 03's independent sum must use the same rule. With this rule the totals equal cost-state `modelUsage` (see above).
- cost-state in the main transcript covers the whole session including workers. `Transcript.cost_usd` = last `cost-state.totalCostUSD` in that transcript (so main only; workers None). Phase 02 should show the session cost from `main.cost_usd` and not add worker costs.
- Token mapping: `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens` -> `cache_read_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens` -> `cache_creation_tokens`.
- Skipped counting: malformed lines, non-object lines, unknown record types, user/assistant records without usable content, unknown assistant/user block shapes, and a cost-state without a numeric totalCostUSD. Known internal types (`INTERNAL_RECORD_TYPES` + prefix `file-history-`, from the plan's list plus `api-request-shape`) are ignored and NOT counted, so the skipped count signals real format drift instead of being always large.
- `errors` counts tool results with `is_error` or `is_denied` (real denials carry both).
- Steps: `index` is 1-based within a transcript; `anchor` = `step-<transcript name>-<index>` (unique across a task since session ids and agent ids are unique). Tool call `text` is `json.dumps(input, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)`. Tool result `text` flattens list content: text blocks joined by newline, other blocks as `[<type> block]`. Tool results also get `tool_name` copied from their call. Non-text user blocks (e.g. image) become a `user` step `[<type> block]`. `redacted_thinking` is treated as `thinking`. Thinking text is empty in current transcripts (redacted), kept as an empty `thinking` step.
- System step text: `content` if a non-empty string, else the `subtype`; non-empty `hookErrors` are appended as JSON.
- Timestamps: `first_timestamp`/`last_timestamp` are min/max of step timestamps (ISO strings compare correctly).
- Worker linking searches tool calls in main and all workers of the session (covers nested workers), by `.meta.json` `toolUseId`, regardless of tool name. Missing/malformed meta -> worker unlinked, `actor` falls back to agent_type then `agent-<id>`.
- Ordering: sessions by main first timestamp (no timestamp last, then by id); workers likewise by first timestamp. Documents: plan.md, status.md, summary.md, then sorted phases/*.md, then sorted progress/*.md.
- A missing main `<session-id>.jsonl` gives an empty main Transcript (still lists workers).

## Interface deviations from the brief
- None in names/fields. Additions: `load_task` raises `FileNotFoundError` if the task directory does not exist (phase 02's main() should catch it and exit non-zero). Helper `TimelineBuilder` class and module constants `INTERNAL_RECORD_TYPES`, `TASK_DOCUMENT_NAMES`, `TASK_DOCUMENT_FOLDERS` exist but are internal.

## Next step / remaining work
- None for 01a. Phase 02 adds rendering and `main()` on top of `load_task`.

## Decisions and assumptions
- Section ids: `overview`, `errors`, `documents`, `transcript-<name>` for each session main (name = session id) and worker (name = agent-<id>); steps use `Step.anchor`. With no sessions, a `sessions` section says "No transcripts were found in logs/.".
- Cost: sum of `main.cost_usd` over sessions; worker costs not added; "n/a" when none.
- Duration: per session, earliest to latest timestamp across main and workers; overview duration = sum of session durations (sessions are separate runs, possibly days apart, so first-to-last over the task would mislead).
- Tokens: overview shows the total of the four counts plus a wide card with the breakdown; sessions show session (main+workers) and orchestrator-only totals; workers show their own.
- Collapsing: `collapsible_text` shows text whole in `<pre>` when short; otherwise `<details>` whose summary shows the first N lines (3 for tool input/output, 12 for user/assistant/system text), capped at N*200 characters so a single huge line also collapses; the body holds exactly the rest, so preview + rest = whole text (tested). Preview uses `<span class="mono preview">` because `<pre>` is not allowed inside `<summary>`.
- Thinking: always collapsed; empty thinking (redacted in current transcripts) shows "(thinking text not recorded)".
- Errors list: one item per errored/denied tool result: link "<actor> · step N" to the step anchor, badge (denied wins over error since real denials have both flags), tool name, first non-empty line of the result (CSS ellipsis; the full text is in the step).
- Tool calls link to their result ("result #N"); the Agent call links to the worker section; the worker header links back ("started by: step #N (Agent)"), or says "not found" when unlinked.
- Generated date: local time, `%Y-%m-%d %H:%M %Z`; `render_report` takes `generated` so tests are deterministic.
- `main` catches OSError on load (FileNotFoundError included) -> "could not read the task", and OSError on write -> "could not write the report"; both exit 1, message on stderr. Success prints the path (as given + /report.html) on stdout.
- Added `<meta name="color-scheme" content="light dark">` so form controls/scrollbars follow the theme.

## Next step / remaining work
- None for 02a. Phase 03 (if any) can do the browser check and the independent token sum against `message.usage`.

## Decisions and assumptions
- Chose cb353a49 (finished session, 2 workers) and a6c2b2e1 (small, no workers). Did not use e1650fd1 (live orchestrator).
- The raw string `"type":"tool_use"` appears more often than real calls because `api-request-blob` records replay earlier assistant messages. Checked: in every file the blob tool_use ids are a subset of the `assistant` tool_use ids and blob messages carry no `usage`, so counting only `assistant` records is correct (and matches the parser, which ignores `api-request-blob`).
- a6c2b2e1 turned out to be degenerate: 2 user records (a `/poe:poe plan` invocation), 6 `api-request` records, no `assistant` records, cost-state 0. The report shows it with 2 user steps, 0 tool calls, 0 tokens, cost $0.0000, duration 0s. Correct for the data; kept as an edge case.

## Observations (not failures)
- Token totals vs cost-state: for 7234187e the deduped sum equals cost-state `modelUsage` exactly (as 01a found). For cb353a49 it does not: cost-state `modelUsage` has `claude-haiku-5-5` input 73,731 / output 2,670 (side queries, no assistant records) and `claude-opus-5-5` input 6,912 / output 36,372 / cache read 2,898,174 / cache creation 173,301, versus the transcripts' 98 / 36,098 / 2,210,558 / 165,611. So cost-state counts API calls that are not written as assistant records. The report's tokens (from transcripts) and its cost (from cost-state, $2.3316 for cb353a49) therefore come from different scopes; the token figures can be lower than what was billed. The acceptance criterion (match a sum over `message.usage` in the same files) is met. Possibly worth a one-line note in the report or README; out of scope for this unit.
- Session duration uses step timestamps only, so a6c2b2e1 shows 0s although its api-request records span 09:49-09:51.

Do not split it into phases and do not edit implementation files. - `split [plan-path]`: split a reviewed `plan.md` into phases and work units, then stop for user approval. - `execute [plan-path]`: execute a plan that has been split and approved. If the plan has no phase briefs yet, say so and ask the user to run `split` first.
- When a mode is given no path, use the unambiguous active plan in `poe/`; otherwise ask for the path.
- `resume [plan-path]`: read `status.md` and the progress notes, compare them with the actual files, tests, and git diff, reconcile discrepancies, and continue only incomplete approved work. When it finishes or stops, write `summary.md` as in Execute mode step 9.
- For small, single-file or tightly coupled changes, recommend doing the work directly instead of forcing subagents. If the user still requests orchestration, comply.

## Files

Everything for one task lives in `poe/<short-task-slug>/`, relative to the current working directory (the directory the session was started in, even if that is not the repository root). Create `poe/` and the task directory if they do not exist (`mkdir -p`). Never overwrite the directory of a different task; pick a distinct slug.

```
poe/<short-task-slug>/
  plan.md              what will be built and why (written by `plan`)
  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units (written by `split`)
  status.md            state of every phase and unit, and the next action
  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker during execution
  summary.md           execution summary, overwritten at the end of each `execute`/`resume` run
  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts of each session that worked on this task
    <session-id>.jsonl         main (orchestrator) transcript
    subagents/agent-*.jsonl    worker transcripts
    subagents/agent-*.meta.json
```

## Plan mode: write `plan.md`

1. Inspect only the relevant repository context, instructions, tests, and environment. Use read-only exploration subagents when exploration would otherwise overwhelm parent context.
2. Write `poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md` from what the user wants to do: objective, scope and exclusions, assumptions, the approach, acceptance criteria, risks, and how the result will be tested or validated. This file describes the work itself; it does not mention phases, subagents, or token budgets.
3. **Do not overengineer.** Plan the simplest approach that meets the stated requirements:
   - Build only what the user asked for. No speculative features, configuration options, plugin systems, or abstraction layers added "for later".
   - Prefer the tools, libraries, and patterns the repository already uses over introducing new ones.
   - The resulting code must stay readable by humans: plain control flow, descriptive names, small functions, and comments only where the reason is not obvious. Prefer a few clear lines over a clever one.
   - Keep the process as light as the code. Do not add steps, files, or reviews that the task does not need.
4. Do not silently choose a major architecture if it needs user approval; ask before writing it into the plan.
5. Show a short summary of the plan, state the path of `plan.md`, and stop. The user reviews the file and may ask for changes; apply requested changes to `plan.md` and stop again. Do not proceed to the split until the user runs `split`.

## Split mode: split `plan.md` into phases and work units

Read `plan.md` as it currently is on disk, including any edits the user made by hand. If phase briefs already exist from an earlier split and no unit has been started, replace them; if work has started, ask before changing them.

1. Split the plan into ordered **phases** with explicit dependencies, and each phase into **work units**. One work unit is handled by one fresh subagent and must be independently verifiable.
2. Size each unit so that one subagent can finish it in roughly 100k tokens of context, counting the files it must read, the edits, and the test output. Split a unit that looks larger; merge units that are trivially small. Use as few phases and units as the work actually needs.
3. Identify which units can run in parallel because they do not modify overlapping files or share mutable state. Run dependent or overlapping units sequentially, or isolate them in separate git worktrees and integrate deliberately.
4. Write one brief per phase at `phases/NN-name.md`. For each unit give: goal, inputs and files to read, files it may change, explicit non-goals, exact verification commands, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path.
5. Write `status.md` listing every phase and unit as pending.
6. Show the phase split, state where the files are, and ask for approval. Do not start implementation in `split` mode.

## Execute mode

1. Read `plan.md`, `status.md`, and the brief of the next phase. Verify that the plan is approved and the prerequisites of the next unit are satisfied.
2. Dispatch a **fresh non-fork subagent** for each work unit. Prefer the `poe:phase-worker` subagent bundled with this plugin (if it is not available under that name, look for `phase-worker`); otherwise use an appropriate built-in agent capable of doing the work. Do not use a fork for routine delegation: it inherits the parent's history.
3. Give the worker only: the goal, the phase-brief path, necessary repository paths, interfaces and dependencies, allowed files, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. Do not paste the parent conversation or large tool output.
4. The worker implements or analyzes only its assigned unit and runs the relevant tests. It writes its progress notes **as it works**, not only at the end: it updates the notes file after each meaningful step, so that the notes are current if the worker stops, is compacted, or fails.
5. Run independent, non-overlapping units in parallel only when safe. Never silently overwrite another agent's edits.
6. Check the worker's claims against the actual files, test outputs, and git diff. For significant or high-risk changes, use a separate fresh-context reviewer before marking the unit verified. Fix or reassign failed units with a new scoped brief.
7. Update `status.md` after every unit. Keep the parent response concise: phase, tests, blockers, and next action. Reference files rather than returning large transcripts.
8. Move to the next approved phase after verification. Stop for genuinely ambiguous requirements, security-sensitive changes, destructive operations, deployments, or decisions beyond the approved plan.
9. At completion, run integration tests, review the full change set, and report what passed, what remains uncertain, and where the files were saved. Write the same report to `summary.md` (what passed, what remains uncertain, blockers, and file locations), overwriting any earlier one; `status.md` stays the source of truth for state.

## Logs

At the end of every mode (`plan`, `split`, `execute`, `resume`), and whenever execution stops for user input, copy this session's transcripts into the task directory:

```sh
dest="poe/<short-task-slug>/logs/b046f754-91e1-4663-9cd7-ce287d366b60"
src=$(find "${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects" -name "b046f754-91e1-4663-9cd7-ce287d366b60.jsonl" | head -1)
mkdir -p "$dest"
cp "$src" "$dest/"
[ -d "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" ] && cp -R "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" "$dest/"
```

- Re-running it overwrites the earlier copy with the current, longer transcript, so repeating it is safe.
- The copy is a snapshot: anything after it (such as the final reply of the turn) is missing until the next copy.
- If the transcript cannot be found (`src` is empty or `cp` fails), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing log never blocks the task.

## Progress notes

Each worker keeps one file, `progress/NN-unit.md`, and updates it throughout its run. `resume` and any follow-on worker rely on it, so it must make sense to an agent that has seen nothing else.

```
# Unit ID: status (in progress | done | partial | blocked)
## Done so far
## Files changed or produced
## Tests run and observed results
## Decisions and assumptions
## Next step / remaining work
```

When a unit is handed to a second worker, that worker reads the existing notes and continues in the same file.

## Context policy: about 100k tokens per agent

- 100k is a **soft target**, not a hard limit. Never claim to know exact token usage unless a tool reports it; otherwise judge by how much has been read and produced.
- Hand over at a natural boundary, not at a number. When a worker nears the target, it finishes the step it is in the middle of (an edit in flight, a test run, a debugging thread that is about to resolve), then brings its progress notes up to date and returns `partial`. Going moderately over the target to finish a coherent step is correct; stopping mid-edit to respect the number is not.
- The orchestrator then starts a **fresh non-fork worker** for the remainder, pointed at the progress notes. Do not keep resuming a bloated agent.
- Prefer this file-based handover to automatic compaction. Automatic compaction is a backstop: it can fire in the middle of a step and loses detail, which is why the notes are kept current throughout.
- Keep the parent lean: orchestration and short summaries only; delegate large searches, code reading, and log interpretation.
- Avoid unnecessary parallelism, repeated repository scans, duplicate reading, and verbose inter-agent messages.

## Constraints

- Follow the project's `CLAUDE.md`, security practices, and user instructions.
- Implement what the plan says and no more. Keep code simple and readable by humans; do not add abstractions or features the plan does not call for.
- Preserve existing user changes; check git status before edits. Do not commit, push, merge, delete, or deploy without authorization.
- Never infer that a test passed without observing its result. Preserve unknowns rather than fabricating evidence.
- If an agent has no delegation tool, say so and execute sequentially using the same file-based phase structure.

#12 · 2026-11-17T14:59:47.651Z · tool result · Bash · error
Exit code 1 # Plan: Inspect-style `report.html` for a poe task show the rest (10,840 more characters)
## Objective

Turn what poe saves for a task in `poe/<task-slug>/` (plan, status, phase briefs, progress notes, summary, and the copied Claude Code transcripts in `logs/`) into one self-contained `poe/<task-slug>/report.html`. The page, styled after the Inspect AI log viewer, lets a person follow, step by step, what the orchestrator and each worker did: prompts, replies, tool calls and their results, errors, and token and cost totals.

The page has two later uses, which shape it now but are not built here:

- **Stacktrace:** when a run goes wrong, the errors and denied tool calls can be found quickly and traced back to the step that caused them.
- **LLM-as-judge input:** all content is plain, server-rendered HTML text (no data hidden in JavaScript), so a model can be given the file as is.

## Scope

In scope:

- `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py` (new): a Python 3 script using only the standard library. `python3 poe_report.py poe/<task-slug>` reads that task directory and writes `poe/<task-slug>/report.html`.
- `tests/test_poe_report.py` and `tests/fixtures/` (new): `unittest` tests against a small, hand-written transcript fixture.
- `skills/poe/SKILL.md`:
  - a new `report [task-path]` mode that (re)builds the report;
  - an automatic report build at the end of `execute` and `resume`, right after the log copy;
  - `report.html` added to the Files layout.
- `README.md`: the `report` mode, `report.html` in the layout, how to run the script by hand, and gitignore advice.

Out of scope:

- The LLM-as-judge itself, scoring, and any JSON or other machine-readable export.
- Converting to Inspect AI's `.eval` log format, or depending on `inspect-ai` or any other pip package.
- A live server, auto-refresh, search boxes, charts, or comparing several tasks.
- Fixing the open log-copy permission problem from `plans/poe-dir-and-logs/` (the copy is refused under restrictive permission modes). The report works with whatever logs exist.
- Rendering Markdown to HTML. The task's `.md` files are shown as preformatted text.

## Assumptions

- The layout is the one in the current `SKILL.md`: `poe/<slug>/{plan.md, status.md, summary.md, phases/*.md, progress/*.md, logs/<session-id>/<session-id>.jsonl, logs/<session-id>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl, agent-<id>.meta.json}`. Any of these may be missing.
- The Claude Code transcript format is not documented and changes between versions. Observed on this machine (Claude Code 2.1.326):
  - Each line is a JSON record with a `type`. The records that matter are `user` and `assistant` (with `message.content` blocks `text`, `thinking`, `tool_use`, `tool_result`), `system`, and `cost-state` (`totalCostUSD`, `totalDuration`, `modelUsage`). Other types (`api-request`, `api-request-blob`, `attachment`, `atis-latch`, `session-owner`, `queue-operation`, `last-prompt`, `tools-used`, ...) are internal and skipped.
  - `assistant` records carry `timestamp`, `message.model`, and `message.usage` (input, output, cache read and cache creation tokens).
  - `user` records with a `tool_result` carry `toolUseResult`, may carry `toolDenialKind` for a refused call, and the block may have `is_error`.
  - A worker's `agent-<id>.meta.json` holds `agentType`, `description` and `toolUseId`. `toolUseId` matches the `id` of the `Agent` `tool_use` block in the main transcript, which is how a worker is linked to the step that started it.
- Records are shown in file order (the order Claude Code appended them). The `parentUuid` tree is not reconstructed.
- `python3` (3.9 or later) is available wherever poe runs. This machine has 3.12.
- Skills substitute `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}`, so `SKILL.md` can call the bundled script by its path. **To be confirmed during implementation** against the skills documentation; otherwise use `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`.

## Approach

### 1. The script, `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`

One file of small functions, in three steps.

**Load.** Read the task's `.md` files as text. For each `logs/<session-id>/` folder, parse the main `.jsonl` and each `subagents/agent-*.jsonl` with its `.meta.json`. A line that is not valid JSON, or a record without the expected fields, is counted and skipped; it never stops the script.

**Build a simple timeline per transcript.** Turn the useful records into a flat list of steps, each with: time, actor (orchestrator or worker name), kind, and text. The kinds are:

- user prompt
- assistant text
- thinking (collapsed)
- tool call (tool name and its input)
- tool result (output; marked as an error when `is_error` or `toolDenialKind` is set)
- system or hook message

Each tool call is paired with its result by `tool_use_id`. An `Agent` tool call links to the worker's own timeline. Per transcript, and in total, the script adds up tokens, the number of tool calls and errors, the model or models, the duration (first to last timestamp), and the cost from `cost-state` when present.

**Render.** Write one HTML file with inline CSS and no external resources, so it opens offline and can be attached or emailed as a single file. The layout follows Inspect's viewer:

- **Sidebar:** anchor links to the overview, the errors list, the task documents, and each session, with its workers indented under it.
- **Overview cards:** the task slug, the date generated, the status table from `status.md` as written, the number of sessions and workers, tool calls, errors, tokens, cost, and duration.
- **Errors:** every errored or denied tool call across all transcripts, each with its tool name, a one-line excerpt, and a link to the step. This is the stacktrace view.
- **Task documents:** `plan.md`, `status.md`, `summary.md`, phase briefs and progress notes, each in a collapsible `<details>` block of preformatted text.
- **Session timelines:** one section per session, ordered by first timestamp, then one per worker. Each step is a row with a coloured left border by kind, a timestamp, and its content. Tool inputs and outputs are in monospace inside `<details>`, with the first few lines visible. Error rows are red. A worker section starts with its type, description, model and totals, and links back to the step that started it.
- Light and dark colours through `prefers-color-scheme`. `<details>` handles collapsing, so the page needs no JavaScript.

All text from the logs goes through `html.escape`, because transcripts contain arbitrary file contents and command output. Content is never truncated in the HTML. Long blocks are collapsed, not cut, so the file stays complete for a stacktrace or a judge.

If `logs/` is missing or empty, the report still renders the documents and says that no transcripts were found.

The script prints the path of the written report and exits 0. It exits non-zero only if the task directory does not exist or the report cannot be written.

### 2. Skill changes, `skills/poe/SKILL.md`

- Operating modes gain `report [plan-path]`: copy the logs (as in the Logs section), then run `python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/poe_report.py" poe/<slug>` and give the user the path of the report.
- Execute mode step 9 and `resume`: after writing `summary.md` and copying the logs, build the report the same way. A failure is reported in the reply and in `summary.md` but never blocks the task, the same rule as for log copies.
- Files layout: add `report.html   step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/`.

### 3. README

Add the `report` mode to the usage list, `report.html` to the layout, the manual command `python3 <plugin>/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py poe/<slug>`, and a note that `report.html` contains the full transcripts, so it belongs in `.gitignore` alongside `logs/`.

## Acceptance criteria

- `python3 -m unittest discover tests` passes. The tests cover:
  - parsing the fixture: the expected number of steps, tool calls paired with their results, an error flagged, and a worker linked by `toolUseId`;
  - skipping unknown record types and malformed lines without crashing;
  - HTML escaping: a `<script>` string in a tool result appears escaped in the output;
  - a task directory without `logs/` still produces a report.
- Running the script on a real task (the copied transcripts of a plan, split and execute run, as in `plans/poe-dir-and-logs` unit 02a) writes a `report.html` that opens in a browser and shows:
  - all sessions and the worker;
  - every tool call, in order, with its result;
  - the denied log-copy commands, listed in the Errors section;
  - token totals that match a separate sum over `message.usage` in the same files.
- The report opens with no network access (no external URLs in the file).
- `SKILL.md` documents the `report` mode and the automatic build. `README.md` matches it. `claude plugin validate .` still passes.
- `/poe:poe report <task>` run with `claude --plugin-dir .` in a scratch directory produces `poe/<slug>/report.html`.

## Risks

- **Transcript format changes.** The parser relies on undocumented fields. Mitigation: read fields defensively, skip what is unknown, and show the count of skipped records in the overview, so silent loss is visible.
- **Logs are often missing** because of the open log-copy permission problem. Mitigation: the report degrades to documents only and says why. Fixing the copy is a separate task.
- **Permission prompts.** Running `python3` on a script outside the working directory may need approval in restrictive modes. Mitigation: the same "report and carry on" rule as for logs. The README gives the manual command.
- **Sensitive data.** The report contains everything in the transcripts. Mitigation: gitignore advice. Nothing is uploaded.
- **Large files.** A long execute run can produce several MB of transcripts, and so a large HTML file. Acceptable for a local file. Collapsed `<details>` keep it usable in a browser.

## Testing and validation

1. Unit tests: `python3 -m unittest discover tests`, using a small, hand-written fixture in `tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/` with a main transcript, one worker transcript and meta file, one errored tool call, one unknown record type, and one malformed line. The fixture holds no real session data.
2. Real data: copy the transcripts of a real poe run into a scratch `poe/<slug>/logs/` (outside the repository, never committed), run the script, and check the report in a browser against the acceptance criteria above. Check the token totals with a short independent `python3` sum over the same files.
3. End to end: `claude --plugin-dir .` in a scratch directory, run `/poe:poe report <slug>` on that scratch task, and confirm that `report.html` is written and the skill reports its path.
4. Static checks: `grep -nE '(src|href)="https?:' report.html` finds no external resources (escaped URLs inside log text don't match), and `claude plugin validate .` passes.

#13 · 2026-11-17T14:59:48.262Z · tool result · Read
1 --- 2 name: poe 3 description: Plan, decompose, and execute complex coding or analysis tasks in phases using fresh-context subagents and file-based progress notes. Use when the user explicitly requests staged multi-agent work, context-conscious orchestration, or continuation of an existing phase plan.show the rest (11,051 more characters)
4	argument-hint: "[plan|split|execute|resume] [task description or plan path]"
5	disable-model-invocation: true
6	---
7	
8	# Phase-Orchestrated Work
9	
10	User request: $ARGUMENTS
11	
12	You are the **orchestrator**. Keep the parent conversation focused on decisions, dependency management, integration, and verification. Delegate bounded implementation or research to fresh-context subagents rather than accumulating their intermediate work in the parent context.
13	
14	## Operating modes
15	
16	- `plan <task>` (or a task description without a mode): write `plan.md` only, then stop so the user can review it. Do not split it into phases and do not edit implementation files.
17	- `split [plan-path]`: split a reviewed `plan.md` into phases and work units, then stop for user approval. Do not edit implementation files.
18	- `execute [plan-path]`: execute a plan that has been split and approved. If the plan has no phase briefs yet, say so and ask the user to run `split` first.
19	- When a mode is given no path, use the unambiguous active plan in `poe/`; otherwise ask for the path.
20	- `resume [plan-path]`: read `status.md` and the progress notes, compare them with the actual files, tests, and git diff, reconcile discrepancies, and continue only incomplete approved work. When it finishes or stops, write `summary.md` as in Execute mode step 9.
21	- For small, single-file or tightly coupled changes, recommend doing the work directly instead of forcing subagents. If the user still requests orchestration, comply.
22	
23	## Files
24	
25	Everything for one task lives in `poe/<short-task-slug>/`, relative to the current working directory (the directory the session was started in, even if that is not the repository root). Create `poe/` and the task directory if they do not exist (`mkdir -p`). Never overwrite the directory of a different task; pick a distinct slug.
26	
27	```
28	poe/<short-task-slug>/
29	  plan.md              what will be built and why (written by `plan`)
30	  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units (written by `split`)
31	  status.md            state of every phase and unit, and the next action
32	  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker during execution
33	  summary.md           execution summary, overwritten at the end of each `execute`/`resume` run
34	  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts of each session that worked on this task
35	    <session-id>.jsonl         main (orchestrator) transcript
36	    subagents/agent-*.jsonl    worker transcripts
37	    subagents/agent-*.meta.json
38	```
39	
40	## Plan mode: write `plan.md`
41	
42	1. Inspect only the relevant repository context, instructions, tests, and environment. Use read-only exploration subagents when exploration would otherwise overwhelm parent context.
43	2. Write `poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md` from what the user wants to do: objective, scope and exclusions, assumptions, the approach, acceptance criteria, risks, and how the result will be tested or validated. This file describes the work itself; it does not mention phases, subagents, or token budgets.
44	3. **Do not overengineer.** Plan the simplest approach that meets the stated requirements:
45	   - Build only what the user asked for. No speculative features, configuration options, plugin systems, or abstraction layers added "for later".
46	   - Prefer the tools, libraries, and patterns the repository already uses over introducing new ones.
47	   - The resulting code must stay readable by humans: plain control flow, descriptive names, small functions, and comments only where the reason is not obvious. Prefer a few clear lines over a clever one.
48	   - Keep the process as light as the code. Do not add steps, files, or reviews that the task does not need.
49	4. Do not silently choose a major architecture if it needs user approval; ask before writing it into the plan.
50	5. Show a short summary of the plan, state the path of `plan.md`, and stop. The user reviews the file and may ask for changes; apply requested changes to `plan.md` and stop again. Do not proceed to the split until the user runs `split`.
51	
52	## Split mode: split `plan.md` into phases and work units
53	
54	Read `plan.md` as it currently is on disk, including any edits the user made by hand. If phase briefs already exist from an earlier split and no unit has been started, replace them; if work has started, ask before changing them.
55	
56	1. Split the plan into ordered **phases** with explicit dependencies, and each phase into **work units**. One work unit is handled by one fresh subagent and must be independently verifiable.
57	2. Size each unit so that one subagent can finish it in roughly 100k tokens of context, counting the files it must read, the edits, and the test output. Split a unit that looks larger; merge units that are trivially small. Use as few phases and units as the work actually needs.
58	3. Identify which units can run in parallel because they do not modify overlapping files or share mutable state. Run dependent or overlapping units sequentially, or isolate them in separate git worktrees and integrate deliberately.
59	4. Write one brief per phase at `phases/NN-name.md`. For each unit give: goal, inputs and files to read, files it may change, explicit non-goals, exact verification commands, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path.
60	5. Write `status.md` listing every phase and unit as pending.
61	6. Show the phase split, state where the files are, and ask for approval. Do not start implementation in `split` mode.
62	
63	## Execute mode
64	
65	1. Read `plan.md`, `status.md`, and the brief of the next phase. Verify that the plan is approved and the prerequisites of the next unit are satisfied.
66	2. Dispatch a **fresh non-fork subagent** for each work unit. Prefer the `poe:phase-worker` subagent bundled with this plugin (if it is not available under that name, look for `phase-worker`); otherwise use an appropriate built-in agent capable of doing the work. Do not use a fork for routine delegation: it inherits the parent's history.
67	3. Give the worker only: the goal, the phase-brief path, necessary repository paths, interfaces and dependencies, allowed files, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. Do not paste the parent conversation or large tool output.
68	4. The worker implements or analyzes only its assigned unit and runs the relevant tests. It writes its progress notes **as it works**, not only at the end: it updates the notes file after each meaningful step, so that the notes are current if the worker stops, is compacted, or fails.
69	5. Run independent, non-overlapping units in parallel only when safe. Never silently overwrite another agent's edits.
70	6. Check the worker's claims against the actual files, test outputs, and git diff. For significant or high-risk changes, use a separate fresh-context reviewer before marking the unit verified. Fix or reassign failed units with a new scoped brief.
71	7. Update `status.md` after every unit. Keep the parent response concise: phase, tests, blockers, and next action. Reference files rather than returning large transcripts.
72	8. Move to the next approved phase after verification. Stop for genuinely ambiguous requirements, security-sensitive changes, destructive operations, deployments, or decisions beyond the approved plan.
73	9. At completion, run integration tests, review the full change set, and report what passed, what remains uncertain, and where the files were saved. Write the same report to `summary.md` (what passed, what remains uncertain, blockers, and file locations), overwriting any earlier one; `status.md` stays the source of truth for state.
74	
75	## Logs
76	
77	At the end of every mode (`plan`, `split`, `execute`, `resume`), and whenever execution stops for user input, copy this session's transcripts into the task directory:
78	
79	```sh
80	dest="poe/<short-task-slug>/logs/${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}"
81	src=$(find "${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects" -name "${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}.jsonl" | head -1)
82	mkdir -p "$dest"
83	cp "$src" "$dest/"
84	[ -d "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" ] && cp -R "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" "$dest/"
85	```
86	
87	- Re-running it overwrites the earlier copy with the current, longer transcript, so repeating it is safe.
88	- The copy is a snapshot: anything after it (such as the final reply of the turn) is missing until the next copy.
89	- If the transcript cannot be found (`src` is empty or `cp` fails), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing log never blocks the task.
90	
91	## Progress notes
92	
93	Each worker keeps one file, `progress/NN-unit.md`, and updates it throughout its run. `resume` and any follow-on worker rely on it, so it must make sense to an agent that has seen nothing else.
94	
95	```
96	# Unit ID: status (in progress | done | partial | blocked)
97	## Done so far
98	## Files changed or produced
99	## Tests run and observed results
100	## Decisions and assumptions
101	## Next step / remaining work
102	```
103	
104	When a unit is handed to a second worker, that worker reads the existing notes and continues in the same file.
105	
106	## Context policy: about 100k tokens per agent
107	
108	- 100k is a **soft target**, not a hard limit. Never claim to know exact token usage unless a tool reports it; otherwise judge by how much has been read and produced.
109	- Hand over at a natural boundary, not at a number. When a worker nears the target, it finishes the step it is in the middle of (an edit in flight, a test run, a debugging thread that is about to resolve), then brings its progress notes up to date and returns `partial`. Going moderately over the target to finish a coherent step is correct; stopping mid-edit to respect the number is not.
110	- The orchestrator then starts a **fresh non-fork worker** for the remainder, pointed at the progress notes. Do not keep resuming a bloated agent.
111	- Prefer this file-based handover to automatic compaction. Automatic compaction is a backstop: it can fire in the middle of a step and loses detail, which is why the notes are kept current throughout.
112	- Keep the parent lean: orchestration and short summaries only; delegate large searches, code reading, and log interpretation.
113	- Avoid unnecessary parallelism, repeated repository scans, duplicate reading, and verbose inter-agent messages.
114	
115	## Constraints
116	
117	- Follow the project's `CLAUDE.md`, security practices, and user instructions.
118	- Implement what the plan says and no more. Keep code simple and readable by humans; do not add abstractions or features the plan does not call for.
119	- Preserve existing user changes; check git status before edits. Do not commit, push, merge, delete, or deploy without authorization.
120	- Never infer that a test passed without observing its result. Preserve unknowns rather than fabricating evidence.
121	- If an agent has no delegation tool, say so and execute sequentially using the same file-based phase structure.

{ "file_path": "/Users/sbandyopadhyay/projects/SCRIPTS/custom_claude_plugins/poe/poe/poe-report-dashboard/plan.md", "content": "# Plan: Inspect-style `report.html` for a poe task\n\n## Objective\n\nTurn what poe saves for a task in `poe/<task-slug>/` (plan, status, phase briefs, progress notes, summary, and the copied Claude Code transcripts in `logs/`) into one self-contained `poe/<task-slug>/report.html`. The page, styled after the Inspect AI log viewer, lets a person follow, step by step, what the orchestrator and each worker did: prompts, replies, tool calls and their results, errors, show the rest (11,081 more characters)
and token and cost totals.\n\nThe page has two later uses, which shape it now but are not built here:\n\n- **Stacktrace:** when a run goes wrong, errored and denied tool calls can be found quickly and traced back to the step that caused them.\n- **LLM-as-judge input:** all content is plain, server-rendered HTML text (no data hidden in JavaScript), so a model can be given the file as is.\n\n## Scope\n\nIn scope:\n\n- `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py` (new): a Python 3 script using only the standard library. `python3 poe_report.py poe/<task-slug>` reads that task directory and writes `poe/<task-slug>/report.html`.\n- `tests/test_poe_report.py` and `tests/fixtures/` (new): `unittest` tests against a small, hand-written transcript fixture.\n- `skills/poe/SKILL.md`:\n  - a new `report [plan-path]` mode that (re)builds the report;\n  - an automatic report build at the end of `execute` and `resume`, right after the log copy;\n  - `report.html` added to the Files layout.\n- `README.md`: the `report` mode, `report.html` in the layout, how to run the script by hand, and gitignore advice.\n\nOut of scope:\n\n- The LLM-as-judge itself, scoring, and any JSON or other machine-readable export.\n- Converting to Inspect AI's `.eval` log format, or depending on `inspect-ai` or any other pip package. \"Inspect style\" means the look and layout, not the tool.\n- A live server, auto-refresh, search boxes, charts, or comparing several tasks.\n- Changing how logs are copied. The report works with whatever is in `logs/`.\n- Rendering Markdown to HTML. The task's `.md` files are shown as preformatted text.\n- The older draft at `plans/poe-report-dashboard/plan.md` (written before output moved to `poe/`). This file replaces it; it is left untouched for the user to delete.\n\n## Assumptions\n\n- The layout is the one in the current `SKILL.md`: `poe/<slug>/{plan.md, status.md, summary.md, phases/*.md, progress/*.md, logs/<session-id>/<session-id>.jsonl, logs/<session-id>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl, agent-<id>.meta.json}`. Any of these may be missing, and there may be several sessions (one per `plan`, `split`, `execute`, `resume` run).\n- The Claude Code transcript format is not documented and changes between versions. Checked on this machine (Claude Code 2.1.326) against existing transcripts:\n  - Each line is a JSON record with a `type`. The records that matter are `user` and `assistant` (with `message.content` blocks `text`, `thinking`, `tool_use`, `tool_result`), `system`, and `cost-state` (`totalCostUSD`, `totalDuration`, `modelUsage`). Other types (`attachment`, `api-request`, `api-request-blob`, `mode`, `session-owner`, `atis-latch`, `last-prompt`, `ai-title`, `permission-mode`, `tools-used`, `file-history-*`, `queue-operation`, ...) are internal and skipped.\n  - `assistant` records carry `timestamp`, `message.model`, and `message.usage` (input, output, cache read and cache creation tokens).\n  - `user` records holding a `tool_result` block may carry `toolUseResult` and `permissionDecision` (`decision`, `source`). The block itself may have `is_error: true`.\n  - A worker's `agent-<id>.meta.json` holds `agentType`, `description` and `toolUseId`. `toolUseId` matches the `id` of the `Agent` `tool_use` block in the main transcript, which is how a worker is linked to the step that started it.\n- A tool result counts as an error when `is_error` is true, or as denied when `permissionDecision.decision` is present and not `accept`. Both are read defensively.\n- Records are shown in file order (the order Claude Code appended them). The `parentUuid` tree is not reconstructed.\n- `python3` (3.9 or later) is available wherever poe runs. This machine has 3.12.\n- Skills substitute `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}`, so `SKILL.md` can call the bundled script by its path. **To be confirmed during implementation** against the skills documentation; otherwise use `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`.\n\n## Approach\n\n### 1. The script, `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`\n\nOne file of small functions, in three steps.\n\n**Load.** Read the task's `.md` files as text. For each `logs/<session-id>/` folder, parse the main `.jsonl` and each `subagents/agent-*.jsonl` with its `.meta.json`. A line that is not valid JSON, or a record without the expected fields, is counted and skipped; it never stops the script.\n\n**Build a simple timeline per transcript.** Turn the useful records into a flat list of steps, each with: time, actor (orchestrator or worker name), kind, and text. The kinds are:\n\n- user prompt\n- assistant text\n- thinking (collapsed)\n- tool call (tool name and its input)\n- tool result (output; marked as an error or denial as above)\n- system or hook message\n\nEach tool call is paired with its result by `tool_use_id`. An `Agent` tool call links to the worker's own timeline. Per transcript, and in total, the script adds up tokens, the number of tool calls and errors, the model or models, the duration (first to last timestamp), and the cost from the last `cost-state` record when present.\n\n**Render.** Write one HTML file with inline CSS and no external resources, so it opens offline and can be attached or emailed as a single file. The layout follows Inspect's viewer:\n\n- **Sidebar:** anchor links to the overview, the errors list, the task documents, and each session, with its workers indented under it.\n- **Overview cards:** the task slug, the date generated, the status table from `status.md` as written, the number of sessions and workers, tool calls, errors, tokens, cost, duration, and the number of skipped records.\n- **Errors:** every errored or denied tool call across all transcripts, each with its tool name, a one-line excerpt, and a link to the step. This is the stacktrace view.\n- **Task documents:** `plan.md`, `status.md`, `summary.md`, phase briefs and progress notes, each in a collapsible `<details>` block of preformatted text.\n- **Session timelines:** one section per session, ordered by first timestamp, then one per worker. Each step is a row with a coloured left border by kind, a timestamp, and its content. Tool inputs and outputs are in monospace inside `<details>`, with the first few lines visible. Error rows are red. A worker section starts with its type, description, model and totals, and links back to the step that started it.\n- Light and dark colours through `prefers-color-scheme`. `<details>` handles collapsing, so the page needs no JavaScript.\n\nAll text from the logs goes through `html.escape`, because transcripts contain arbitrary file contents and command output. Content is never truncated in the HTML. Long blocks are collapsed, not cut, so the file stays complete for a stacktrace or a judge.\n\nIf `logs/` is missing or empty, the report still renders the documents and says that no transcripts were found.\n\nThe script prints the path of the written report and exits 0. It exits non-zero only if the task directory does not exist or the report cannot be written.\n\n### 2. Skill changes, `skills/poe/SKILL.md`\n\n- Operating modes gain `report [plan-path]`: copy the logs (as in the Logs section), then run `python3 \"${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/poe_report.py\" poe/<slug>` and give the user the path of the report.\n- Execute mode step 9 and `resume`: after writing `summary.md` and copying the logs, build the report the same way. A failure is reported in the reply but never blocks the task, the same rule as for log copies.\n- Files layout: add `report.html   step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/`.\n\n### 3. README\n\nAdd the `report` mode to the usage list, `report.html` to the layout, the manual command `python3 <plugin>/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py poe/<slug>`, and a note that `report.html` contains the full transcripts, so it belongs in `.gitignore` alongside `logs/`.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- `python3 -m unittest discover tests` passes. The tests cover:\n  - parsing the fixture: the expected number of steps, tool calls paired with their results, an error flagged, and a worker linked by `toolUseId`;\n  - skipping unknown record types and malformed lines without crashing;\n  - HTML escaping: a `<script>` string in a tool result appears escaped in the output;\n  - a task directory without `logs/` still produces a report.\n- Running the script on real transcripts (a poe session from this project, including one with workers, copied into a scratch `poe/<slug>/logs/`) writes a `report.html` that opens in a browser and shows:\n  - all sessions and their workers;\n  - every tool call, in order, with its result;\n  - every `is_error` tool result, listed in the Errors section;\n  - token totals that match a separate sum over `message.usage` in the same files.\n- The report opens with no network access (no external URLs in the file).\n- `SKILL.md` documents the `report` mode and the automatic build. `README.md` matches it. `claude plugin validate .` still passes.\n- `/poe:poe report <slug>` run with `claude --plugin-dir .` in a scratch directory produces `poe/<slug>/report.html`.\n\n## Risks\n\n- **Transcript format changes.** The parser relies on undocumented fields. Mitigation: read fields defensively, skip what is unknown, and show the count of skipped records in the overview, so silent loss is visible.\n- **Missing logs.** A log copy can fail (for example, refused under a restrictive permission mode). Mitigation: the report degrades to documents only and says why.\n- **Permission prompts.** Running `python3` on a script outside the working directory may need approval in restrictive modes. Mitigation: the same \"report and carry on\" rule as for logs. The README gives the manual command.\n- **Sensitive data.** The report contains everything in the transcripts, including file contents and command output. Mitigation: gitignore advice. Nothing is uploaded.\n- **Large files.** A long execute run can produce several MB of transcripts, and so a large HTML file. Acceptable for a local file. Collapsed `<details>` keep it usable in a browser.\n\n## Testing and validation\n\n1. Unit tests: `python3 -m unittest discover tests`, using a small, hand-written fixture in `tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/` with a main transcript, one worker transcript and meta file, one errored tool call, one unknown record type, and one malformed line. The fixture holds no real session data.\n2. Real data: copy the transcripts of a real poe session from `~/.claude/projects/` into a scratch `poe/<slug>/logs/` (outside the repository, never committed), run the script, and check the report in a browser against the acceptance criteria above. Check the token totals with a short independent `python3` sum over the same files.\n3. End to end: `claude --plugin-dir .` in a scratch directory, run `/poe:poe report <slug>` on that scratch task, and confirm that `report.html` is written and the skill reports its path.\n4. Static checks: `grep -nE '(src|href)=\"https?:' report.html` finds no external resources (escaped URLs inside log text don't match), and `claude plugin validate .` passes.\n"
}
