Base directory for this skill: /Users/sbandyopadhyay/.claude/plugins/cache/poe-tools/poe/72486ab3df24/skills/poe # Phase-Orchestrated Work User request: split @poe/poe-report-dashboard/plan.md You are the **orchestrator**. Keep the parent conversation focused on decisions, dependency management, integration, and verification. Delegate bounded implementation or research to fresh-context subagents rather than accumulating their intermediate work in the parent context. ## Operating modes - `plan <task>` (or a task description without a mode): write `plan.md` only, then stop so the user can review it. Do not split it into phases and do not edit implementation files. - `split [plan-path]`: split a reviewed `plan.md` into phases and work units, then stop for user approval. Do not edit implementation files.show the rest (9,922 more characters)
- `execute [plan-path]`: execute a plan that has been split and approved. If the plan has no phase briefs yet, say so and ask the user to run `split` first.
- When a mode is given no path, use the unambiguous active plan in `poe/`; otherwise ask for the path.
- `resume [plan-path]`: read `status.md` and the progress notes, compare them with the actual files, tests, and git diff, reconcile discrepancies, and continue only incomplete approved work. When it finishes or stops, write `summary.md` as in Execute mode step 9.
- For small, single-file or tightly coupled changes, recommend doing the work directly instead of forcing subagents. If the user still requests orchestration, comply.

## Files

Everything for one task lives in `poe/<short-task-slug>/`, relative to the current working directory (the directory the session was started in, even if that is not the repository root). Create `poe/` and the task directory if they do not exist (`mkdir -p`). Never overwrite the directory of a different task; pick a distinct slug.

```
poe/<short-task-slug>/
  plan.md              what will be built and why (written by `plan`)
  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units (written by `split`)
  status.md            state of every phase and unit, and the next action
  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker during execution
  summary.md           execution summary, overwritten at the end of each `execute`/`resume` run
  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts of each session that worked on this task
    <session-id>.jsonl         main (orchestrator) transcript
    subagents/agent-*.jsonl    worker transcripts
    subagents/agent-*.meta.json
```

## Plan mode: write `plan.md`

1. Inspect only the relevant repository context, instructions, tests, and environment. Use read-only exploration subagents when exploration would otherwise overwhelm parent context.
2. Write `poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md` from what the user wants to do: objective, scope and exclusions, assumptions, the approach, acceptance criteria, risks, and how the result will be tested or validated. This file describes the work itself; it does not mention phases, subagents, or token budgets.
3. **Do not overengineer.** Plan the simplest approach that meets the stated requirements:
   - Build only what the user asked for. No speculative features, configuration options, plugin systems, or abstraction layers added "for later".
   - Prefer the tools, libraries, and patterns the repository already uses over introducing new ones.
   - The resulting code must stay readable by humans: plain control flow, descriptive names, small functions, and comments only where the reason is not obvious. Prefer a few clear lines over a clever one.
   - Keep the process as light as the code. Do not add steps, files, or reviews that the task does not need.
4. Do not silently choose a major architecture if it needs user approval; ask before writing it into the plan.
5. Show a short summary of the plan, state the path of `plan.md`, and stop. The user reviews the file and may ask for changes; apply requested changes to `plan.md` and stop again. Do not proceed to the split until the user runs `split`.

## Split mode: split `plan.md` into phases and work units

Read `plan.md` as it currently is on disk, including any edits the user made by hand. If phase briefs already exist from an earlier split and no unit has been started, replace them; if work has started, ask before changing them.

1. Split the plan into ordered **phases** with explicit dependencies, and each phase into **work units**. One work unit is handled by one fresh subagent and must be independently verifiable.
2. Size each unit so that one subagent can finish it in roughly 100k tokens of context, counting the files it must read, the edits, and the test output. Split a unit that looks larger; merge units that are trivially small. Use as few phases and units as the work actually needs.
3. Identify which units can run in parallel because they do not modify overlapping files or share mutable state. Run dependent or overlapping units sequentially, or isolate them in separate git worktrees and integrate deliberately.
4. Write one brief per phase at `phases/NN-name.md`. For each unit give: goal, inputs and files to read, files it may change, explicit non-goals, exact verification commands, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path.
5. Write `status.md` listing every phase and unit as pending.
6. Show the phase split, state where the files are, and ask for approval. Do not start implementation in `split` mode.

## Execute mode

1. Read `plan.md`, `status.md`, and the brief of the next phase. Verify that the plan is approved and the prerequisites of the next unit are satisfied.
2. Dispatch a **fresh non-fork subagent** for each work unit. Prefer the `poe:phase-worker` subagent bundled with this plugin (if it is not available under that name, look for `phase-worker`); otherwise use an appropriate built-in agent capable of doing the work. Do not use a fork for routine delegation: it inherits the parent's history.
3. Give the worker only: the goal, the phase-brief path, necessary repository paths, interfaces and dependencies, allowed files, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. Do not paste the parent conversation or large tool output.
4. The worker implements or analyzes only its assigned unit and runs the relevant tests. It writes its progress notes **as it works**, not only at the end: it updates the notes file after each meaningful step, so that the notes are current if the worker stops, is compacted, or fails.
5. Run independent, non-overlapping units in parallel only when safe. Never silently overwrite another agent's edits.
6. Check the worker's claims against the actual files, test outputs, and git diff. For significant or high-risk changes, use a separate fresh-context reviewer before marking the unit verified. Fix or reassign failed units with a new scoped brief.
7. Update `status.md` after every unit. Keep the parent response concise: phase, tests, blockers, and next action. Reference files rather than returning large transcripts.
8. Move to the next approved phase after verification. Stop for genuinely ambiguous requirements, security-sensitive changes, destructive operations, deployments, or decisions beyond the approved plan.
9. At completion, run integration tests, review the full change set, and report what passed, what remains uncertain, and where the files were saved. Write the same report to `summary.md` (what passed, what remains uncertain, blockers, and file locations), overwriting any earlier one; `status.md` stays the source of truth for state.

## Logs

At the end of every mode (`plan`, `split`, `execute`, `resume`), and whenever execution stops for user input, copy this session's transcripts into the task directory:

```sh
dest="poe/<short-task-slug>/logs/5d0a8152-f6fe-4c80-be93-3fa426509dc4"
src=$(find "${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects" -name "5d0a8152-f6fe-4c80-be93-3fa426509dc4.jsonl" | head -1)
mkdir -p "$dest"
cp "$src" "$dest/"
[ -d "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" ] && cp -R "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" "$dest/"
```

- Re-running it overwrites the earlier copy with the current, longer transcript, so repeating it is safe.
- The copy is a snapshot: anything after it (such as the final reply of the turn) is missing until the next copy.
- If the transcript cannot be found (`src` is empty or `cp` fails), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing log never blocks the task.

## Progress notes

Each worker keeps one file, `progress/NN-unit.md`, and updates it throughout its run. `resume` and any follow-on worker rely on it, so it must make sense to an agent that has seen nothing else.

```
# Unit ID: status (in progress | done | partial | blocked)
## Done so far
## Files changed or produced
## Tests run and observed results
## Decisions and assumptions
## Next step / remaining work
```

When a unit is handed to a second worker, that worker reads the existing notes and continues in the same file.

## Context policy: about 100k tokens per agent

- 100k is a **soft target**, not a hard limit. Never claim to know exact token usage unless a tool reports it; otherwise judge by how much has been read and produced.
- Hand over at a natural boundary, not at a number. When a worker nears the target, it finishes the step it is in the middle of (an edit in flight, a test run, a debugging thread that is about to resolve), then brings its progress notes up to date and returns `partial`. Going moderately over the target to finish a coherent step is correct; stopping mid-edit to respect the number is not.
- The orchestrator then starts a **fresh non-fork worker** for the remainder, pointed at the progress notes. Do not keep resuming a bloated agent.
- Prefer this file-based handover to automatic compaction. Automatic compaction is a backstop: it can fire in the middle of a step and loses detail, which is why the notes are kept current throughout.
- Keep the parent lean: orchestration and short summaries only; delegate large searches, code reading, and log interpretation.
- Avoid unnecessary parallelism, repeated repository scans, duplicate reading, and verbose inter-agent messages.

## Constraints

- Follow the project's `CLAUDE.md`, security practices, and user instructions.
- Implement what the plan says and no more. Keep code simple and readable by humans; do not add abstractions or features the plan does not call for.
- Preserve existing user changes; check git status before edits. Do not commit, push, merge, delete, or deploy without authorization.
- Never infer that a test passed without observing its result. Preserve unknowns rather than fabricating evidence.
- If an agent has no delegation tool, say so and execute sequentially using the same file-based phase structure.

**Steps.**

1. Create an empty scratch directory with `git init` (so `resume`/`execute` git checks have something to read).
2. In that directory, run three headless sessions against the local plugin, one per mode, e.g.:
   ```sh
   claude -p --plugin-dir /Users/sbandyopadhyay/projects/SCRIPTS/custom_claude_plugins/poe \

3. Inspect `poe/<slug>/`:

4. For each copied main `.jsonl`, `diff` it against the original under `~/.claude/projects/...`: the copy must equal a prefix of the original (only trailing lines missing). Parse each copy with the `python3` one-liner from the plan.
5. If something fails, record exactly what and why. Do not edit `SKILL.md` or `README.md`; report back so the orchestrator can scope a fix.

**Non-goals.** No repository edits, no commits, no cleanup of `~/.claude/projects`. Do not run anything on remote systems.

**Verification commands.**

```

name: poe

argument-hint: "[plan|split|execute|resume] [task description or plan path]"

# Phase-Orchestrated Work

User request: $ARGUMENTS

You are the **orchestrator**. Keep the parent conversation focused on decisions, dependency management, integration, and verification. Delegate bounded implementation or research to fresh-context subagents rather than accumulating their intermediate work in the parent context.

## Operating modes

- `plan <task>` (or a task description without a mode): write `plan.md` only, then stop so the user can review it. Do not split it into phases and do not edit implementation files.
- `split [plan-path]`: split a reviewed `plan.md` into phases and work units, then stop for user approval. Do not edit implementation files.
- `execute [plan-path]`: execute a plan that has been split and approved. If the plan has no phase briefs yet, say so and ask the user to run `split` first.
- When a mode is given no path, use the unambiguous active plan in `poe/`; otherwise ask for the path.

Base directory for this skill: /Users/sbandyopadhyay/.claude/plugins/cache/poe-tools/poe/72486ab3df24/skills/poe # Phase-Orchestrated Work User request: execute @poe/poe-report-dashboard/plan.md You are the **orchestrator**. Keep the parent conversation focused on decisions, dependency management, integration, and verification. Delegate bounded implementation or research to fresh-context subagents rather than accumulating their intermediate work in the parent context. ## Operating modes - `plan <task>` (or a task description without a mode): write `plan.md` only, then stop so the user can review it. Do not split it into phases and do not edit implementation files. - `split [plan-path]`: split a reviewed `plan.md` into phases and work units, then stop for user approval. Do not edit implementation files.show the rest (9,922 more characters)
- `execute [plan-path]`: execute a plan that has been split and approved. If the plan has no phase briefs yet, say so and ask the user to run `split` first.
- When a mode is given no path, use the unambiguous active plan in `poe/`; otherwise ask for the path.
- `resume [plan-path]`: read `status.md` and the progress notes, compare them with the actual files, tests, and git diff, reconcile discrepancies, and continue only incomplete approved work. When it finishes or stops, write `summary.md` as in Execute mode step 9.
- For small, single-file or tightly coupled changes, recommend doing the work directly instead of forcing subagents. If the user still requests orchestration, comply.

## Files

Everything for one task lives in `poe/<short-task-slug>/`, relative to the current working directory (the directory the session was started in, even if that is not the repository root). Create `poe/` and the task directory if they do not exist (`mkdir -p`). Never overwrite the directory of a different task; pick a distinct slug.

```
poe/<short-task-slug>/
  plan.md              what will be built and why (written by `plan`)
  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units (written by `split`)
  status.md            state of every phase and unit, and the next action
  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker during execution
  summary.md           execution summary, overwritten at the end of each `execute`/`resume` run
  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts of each session that worked on this task
    <session-id>.jsonl         main (orchestrator) transcript
    subagents/agent-*.jsonl    worker transcripts
    subagents/agent-*.meta.json
```

## Plan mode: write `plan.md`

1. Inspect only the relevant repository context, instructions, tests, and environment. Use read-only exploration subagents when exploration would otherwise overwhelm parent context.
2. Write `poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md` from what the user wants to do: objective, scope and exclusions, assumptions, the approach, acceptance criteria, risks, and how the result will be tested or validated. This file describes the work itself; it does not mention phases, subagents, or token budgets.
3. **Do not overengineer.** Plan the simplest approach that meets the stated requirements:
   - Build only what the user asked for. No speculative features, configuration options, plugin systems, or abstraction layers added "for later".
   - Prefer the tools, libraries, and patterns the repository already uses over introducing new ones.
   - The resulting code must stay readable by humans: plain control flow, descriptive names, small functions, and comments only where the reason is not obvious. Prefer a few clear lines over a clever one.
   - Keep the process as light as the code. Do not add steps, files, or reviews that the task does not need.
4. Do not silently choose a major architecture if it needs user approval; ask before writing it into the plan.
5. Show a short summary of the plan, state the path of `plan.md`, and stop. The user reviews the file and may ask for changes; apply requested changes to `plan.md` and stop again. Do not proceed to the split until the user runs `split`.

## Split mode: split `plan.md` into phases and work units

Read `plan.md` as it currently is on disk, including any edits the user made by hand. If phase briefs already exist from an earlier split and no unit has been started, replace them; if work has started, ask before changing them.

1. Split the plan into ordered **phases** with explicit dependencies, and each phase into **work units**. One work unit is handled by one fresh subagent and must be independently verifiable.
2. Size each unit so that one subagent can finish it in roughly 100k tokens of context, counting the files it must read, the edits, and the test output. Split a unit that looks larger; merge units that are trivially small. Use as few phases and units as the work actually needs.
3. Identify which units can run in parallel because they do not modify overlapping files or share mutable state. Run dependent or overlapping units sequentially, or isolate them in separate git worktrees and integrate deliberately.
4. Write one brief per phase at `phases/NN-name.md`. For each unit give: goal, inputs and files to read, files it may change, explicit non-goals, exact verification commands, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path.
5. Write `status.md` listing every phase and unit as pending.
6. Show the phase split, state where the files are, and ask for approval. Do not start implementation in `split` mode.

## Execute mode

1. Read `plan.md`, `status.md`, and the brief of the next phase. Verify that the plan is approved and the prerequisites of the next unit are satisfied.
2. Dispatch a **fresh non-fork subagent** for each work unit. Prefer the `poe:phase-worker` subagent bundled with this plugin (if it is not available under that name, look for `phase-worker`); otherwise use an appropriate built-in agent capable of doing the work. Do not use a fork for routine delegation: it inherits the parent's history.
3. Give the worker only: the goal, the phase-brief path, necessary repository paths, interfaces and dependencies, allowed files, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. Do not paste the parent conversation or large tool output.
4. The worker implements or analyzes only its assigned unit and runs the relevant tests. It writes its progress notes **as it works**, not only at the end: it updates the notes file after each meaningful step, so that the notes are current if the worker stops, is compacted, or fails.
5. Run independent, non-overlapping units in parallel only when safe. Never silently overwrite another agent's edits.
6. Check the worker's claims against the actual files, test outputs, and git diff. For significant or high-risk changes, use a separate fresh-context reviewer before marking the unit verified. Fix or reassign failed units with a new scoped brief.
7. Update `status.md` after every unit. Keep the parent response concise: phase, tests, blockers, and next action. Reference files rather than returning large transcripts.
8. Move to the next approved phase after verification. Stop for genuinely ambiguous requirements, security-sensitive changes, destructive operations, deployments, or decisions beyond the approved plan.
9. At completion, run integration tests, review the full change set, and report what passed, what remains uncertain, and where the files were saved. Write the same report to `summary.md` (what passed, what remains uncertain, blockers, and file locations), overwriting any earlier one; `status.md` stays the source of truth for state.

## Logs

At the end of every mode (`plan`, `split`, `execute`, `resume`), and whenever execution stops for user input, copy this session's transcripts into the task directory:

```sh
dest="poe/<short-task-slug>/logs/e1650fd1-5110-41f6-944b-7f0774e47f42"
src=$(find "${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects" -name "e1650fd1-5110-41f6-944b-7f0774e47f42.jsonl" | head -1)
mkdir -p "$dest"
cp "$src" "$dest/"
[ -d "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" ] && cp -R "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" "$dest/"
```

- Re-running it overwrites the earlier copy with the current, longer transcript, so repeating it is safe.
- The copy is a snapshot: anything after it (such as the final reply of the turn) is missing until the next copy.
- If the transcript cannot be found (`src` is empty or `cp` fails), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing log never blocks the task.

## Progress notes

Each worker keeps one file, `progress/NN-unit.md`, and updates it throughout its run. `resume` and any follow-on worker rely on it, so it must make sense to an agent that has seen nothing else.

```
# Unit ID: status (in progress | done | partial | blocked)
## Done so far
## Files changed or produced
## Tests run and observed results
## Decisions and assumptions
## Next step / remaining work
```

When a unit is handed to a second worker, that worker reads the existing notes and continues in the same file.

## Context policy: about 100k tokens per agent

- 100k is a **soft target**, not a hard limit. Never claim to know exact token usage unless a tool reports it; otherwise judge by how much has been read and produced.
- Hand over at a natural boundary, not at a number. When a worker nears the target, it finishes the step it is in the middle of (an edit in flight, a test run, a debugging thread that is about to resolve), then brings its progress notes up to date and returns `partial`. Going moderately over the target to finish a coherent step is correct; stopping mid-edit to respect the number is not.
- The orchestrator then starts a **fresh non-fork worker** for the remainder, pointed at the progress notes. Do not keep resuming a bloated agent.
- Prefer this file-based handover to automatic compaction. Automatic compaction is a backstop: it can fire in the middle of a step and loses detail, which is why the notes are kept current throughout.
- Keep the parent lean: orchestration and short summaries only; delegate large searches, code reading, and log interpretation.
- Avoid unnecessary parallelism, repeated repository scans, duplicate reading, and verbose inter-agent messages.

## Constraints

- Follow the project's `CLAUDE.md`, security practices, and user instructions.
- Implement what the plan says and no more. Keep code simple and readable by humans; do not add abstractions or features the plan does not call for.
- Preserve existing user changes; check git status before edits. Do not commit, push, merge, delete, or deploy without authorization.
- Never infer that a test passed without observing its result. Preserve unknowns rather than fabricating evidence.
- If an agent has no delegation tool, say so and execute sequentially using the same file-based phase structure.

**Interface for phase 02** (plain dataclasses, standard library only; names may be adjusted if recorded in the progress notes):

- `Step`: `index`, `timestamp` (str or None), `kind` (one of `user`, `assistant`, `thinking`, `tool_call`, `tool_result`, `system`), `text`, `tool_name`, `tool_use_id`, `is_error`, `is_denied`, `anchor` (unique HTML id string), `result` (the paired `tool_result` Step for a `tool_call`, else None), `worker` (the linked worker Transcript for an `Agent` call, else None).

- `Session`: `session_id`, `main` (Transcript), `workers` (list of Transcript).
- `Task`: `slug`, `task_dir`, `documents` (ordered list of `(relative_path, text)` for `plan.md`, `status.md`, `summary.md`, `phases/*.md`, `progress/*.md`), `sessions` (ordered by first timestamp), `skipped` (total).
- `load_task(task_dir: Path) -> Task`.

**Rules from the plan.** Skip unknown record types and malformed lines, counting them. Pair calls and results by `tool_use_id`. Link workers via `.meta.json` `toolUseId` regardless of the tool's name (`Agent` or older `Task`). Errors: `is_error` true; denied: `permissionDecision.decision` present and not `accept`.  - **Plan** writes `plan.md` from what you asked for, keeping the approach simple and the code human-readable, then stops. Review the file, edit it or ask for changes, and repeat until you are happy with it.
 - **Split** takes the reviewed `plan.md` and splits it into phases and work units, each sized for one subagent at roughly 100k tokens. It then stops for approval.

 - **Resume** uses the status file and progress notes to continue safely.

**Interface for phase 02** (plain dataclasses, standard library only; names may be adjusted if recorded in the progress notes):

- `Step`: `index`, `timestamp` (str or None), `kind` (one of `user`, `assistant`, `thinking`, `tool_call`, `tool_result`, `system`), `text`, `tool_name`, `tool_use_id`, `is_error`, `is_denied`, `anchor` (unique HTML id string), `result` (the paired `tool_result` Step for a `tool_call`, else None), `worker` (the linked worker Transcript for an `Agent` call, else None).
- `Transcript`: `name` (session id or `agent-<id>`), `actor` (`orchestrator` or the worker's description/type), `agent_type`, `description`, `steps`, `models` (set), token totals (`input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens`, `cache_creation_tokens`), `tool_calls`, `errors`, `first_timestamp`, `last_timestamp`, `cost_usd` (or None), `skipped` (count), `parent_step` (the `Agent` call Step, for workers).
- `Session`: `session_id`, `main` (Transcript), `workers` (list of Transcript).
- `Task`: `slug`, `task_dir`, `documents` (ordered list of `(relative_path, text)` for `plan.md`, `status.md`, `summary.md`, `phases/*.md`, `progress/*.md`), `sessions` (ordered by first timestamp), `skipped` (total).
- `load_task(task_dir: Path) -> Task`.

**Rules from the plan.** Skip unknown record types and malformed lines, counting them. Pair calls and results by `tool_use_id`. Link workers via `.meta.json` `toolUseId` regardless of the tool's name (`Agent` or older `Task`). Errors: `is_error` true; denied: `permissionDecision.decision` present and not `accept`. Keep file order. Never truncate text.

**Check before trusting usage.** Claude Code may write one `assistant` record per content block, each repeating the same `message.id` and `message.usage`. Check this in the real transcripts. If it happens, count usage once per `message.id`, and record the rule in the progress notes (phase 03's independent sum must use the same rule).

**Fixture** (hand-written, no real session data): `tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/` with `plan.md`, `status.md`, one `phases/01-x.md`, one `progress/01a-x.md`, and `logs/<fake-session-id>/` holding a main transcript and `subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl` + `.meta.json`. Include: one `Agent` tool call linked to the worker, one tool result with `is_error: true`, one denied result, one tool result containing `<script>alert(1)</script>` (used by phase 02's escaping test), one unknown record type, one malformed line, and a `cost-state` record.

**Tests** (`unittest`): expected step count; each tool call paired with its result; error and denial flagged; worker linked via `toolUseId`; unknown type and malformed line skipped and counted; token totals; a task dir without `logs/` loads with no sessions.

#7 · 2026-11-17T15:25:26.017Z · tool result · Bash · error
Exit code 1 # Plan: Inspect-style `report.html` for a poe task show the rest (9,975 more characters)
## Objective

Turn what poe saves for a task in `poe/<task-slug>/` (plan, status, phase briefs, progress notes, summary, and the copied Claude Code transcripts in `logs/`) into one self-contained `poe/<task-slug>/report.html`. The page, styled after the Inspect AI log viewer, lets a person follow, step by step, what the orchestrator and each worker did: prompts, replies, tool calls and their results, errors, and token and cost totals.

The page has two later uses, which shape it now but are not built here:

- **Stacktrace:** when a run goes wrong, errored and denied tool calls can be found quickly and traced back to the step that caused them.
- **LLM-as-judge input:** all content is plain, server-rendered HTML text (no data hidden in JavaScript), so a model can be given the file as is.

## Scope

In scope:

- `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py` (new): a Python 3 script using only the standard library. `python3 poe_report.py poe/<task-slug>` reads that task directory and writes `poe/<task-slug>/report.html`.
- `tests/test_poe_report.py` and `tests/fixtures/` (new): `unittest` tests against a small, hand-written transcript fixture.
- `skills/poe/SKILL.md`:
  - a new `report [plan-path]` mode that (re)builds the report;
  - an automatic report build at the end of `execute` and `resume`, right after the log copy;
  - `report.html` added to the Files layout.
- `README.md`: the `report` mode, `report.html` in the layout, how to run the script by hand, and gitignore advice.

Out of scope:

- The LLM-as-judge itself, scoring, and any JSON or other machine-readable export.
- Converting to Inspect AI's `.eval` log format, or depending on `inspect-ai` or any other pip package. "Inspect style" means the look and layout, not the tool.
- A live server, auto-refresh, search boxes, charts, or comparing several tasks.
- Changing how logs are copied. The report works with whatever is in `logs/`.
- Rendering Markdown to HTML. The task's `.md` files are shown as preformatted text.
- The older draft at `plans/poe-report-dashboard/plan.md` (written before output moved to `poe/`). This file replaces it; it is left untouched for the user to delete.

## Assumptions

- The layout is the one in the current `SKILL.md`: `poe/<slug>/{plan.md, status.md, summary.md, phases/*.md, progress/*.md, logs/<session-id>/<session-id>.jsonl, logs/<session-id>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl, agent-<id>.meta.json}`. Any of these may be missing, and there may be several sessions (one per `plan`, `split`, `execute`, `resume` run).
- The Claude Code transcript format is not documented and changes between versions. Checked on this machine (Claude Code 2.1.326) against existing transcripts:
  - Each line is a JSON record with a `type`. The records that matter are `user` and `assistant` (with `message.content` blocks `text`, `thinking`, `tool_use`, `tool_result`), `system`, and `cost-state` (`totalCostUSD`, `totalDuration`, `modelUsage`). Other types (`attachment`, `api-request`, `api-request-blob`, `mode`, `session-owner`, `atis-latch`, `last-prompt`, `ai-title`, `permission-mode`, `tools-used`, `file-history-*`, `queue-operation`, ...) are internal and skipped.
  - `assistant` records carry `timestamp`, `message.model`, and `message.usage` (input, output, cache read and cache creation tokens).
  - `user` records holding a `tool_result` block may carry `toolUseResult` and `permissionDecision` (`decision`, `source`). The block itself may have `is_error: true`.
  - A worker's `agent-<id>.meta.json` holds `agentType`, `description` and `toolUseId`. `toolUseId` matches the `id` of the `Agent` `tool_use` block in the main transcript, which is how a worker is linked to the step that started it.
- A tool result counts as an error when `is_error` is true, or as denied when `permissionDecision.decision` is present and not `accept`. Both are read defensively.
- Records are shown in file order (the order Claude Code appended them). The `parentUuid` tree is not reconstructed.
- `python3` (3.9 or later) is available wherever poe runs. This machine has 3.12.
- Skills substitute `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}`, so `SKILL.md` can call the bundled script by its path. **To be confirmed during implementation** against the skills documentation; otherwise use `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`.

## Approach

### 1. The script, `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`

One file of small functions, in three steps.

**Load.** Read the task's `.md` files as text. For each `logs/<session-id>/` folder, parse the main `.jsonl` and each `subagents/agent-*.jsonl` with its `.meta.json`. A line that is not valid JSON, or a record without the expected fields, is counted and skipped; it never stops the script.

**Build a simple timeline per transcript.** Turn the useful records into a flat list of steps, each with: time, actor (orchestrator or worker name), kind, and text. The kinds are:

- user prompt
- assistant text
- thi

... [1788 characters truncated] ...

efers-color-scheme`. `<details>` handles collapsing, so the page needs no JavaScript.

All text from the logs goes through `html.escape`, because transcripts contain arbitrary file contents and command output. Content is never truncated in the HTML. Long blocks are collapsed, not cut, so the file stays complete for a stacktrace or a judge.

If `logs/` is missing or empty, the report still renders the documents and says that no transcripts were found.

The script prints the path of the written report and exits 0. It exits non-zero only if the task directory does not exist or the report cannot be written.

### 2. Skill changes, `skills/poe/SKILL.md`

- Operating modes gain `report [plan-path]`: copy the logs (as in the Logs section), then run `python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/poe_report.py" poe/<slug>` and give the user the path of the report.
- Execute mode step 9 and `resume`: after writing `summary.md` and copying the logs, build the report the same way. A failure is reported in the reply but never blocks the task, the same rule as for log copies.
- Files layout: add `report.html   step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/`.

### 3. README

Add the `report` mode to the usage list, `report.html` to the layout, the manual command `python3 <plugin>/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py poe/<slug>`, and a note that `report.html` contains the full transcripts, so it belongs in `.gitignore` alongside `logs/`.

## Acceptance criteria

- `python3 -m unittest discover tests` passes. The tests cover:
  - parsing the fixture: the expected number of steps, tool calls paired with their results, an error flagged, and a worker linked by `toolUseId`;
  - skipping unknown record types and malformed lines without crashing;
  - HTML escaping: a `<script>` string in a tool result appears escaped in the output;
  - a task directory without `logs/` still produces a report.
- Running the script on real transcripts (a poe session from this project, including one with workers, copied into a scratch `poe/<slug>/logs/`) writes a `report.html` that opens in a browser and shows:
  - all sessions and their workers;
  - every tool call, in order, with its result;
  - every `is_error` tool result, listed in the Errors section;
  - token totals that match a separate sum over `message.usage` in the same files.
- The report opens with no network access (no external URLs in the file).
- `SKILL.md` documents the `report` mode and the automatic build. `README.md` matches it. `claude plugin validate .` still passes.
- `/poe:poe report <slug>` run with `claude --plugin-dir .` in a scratch directory produces `poe/<slug>/report.html`.

## Risks

- **Transcript format changes.** The parser relies on undocumented fields. Mitigation: read fields defensively, skip what is unknown, and show the count of skipped records in the overview, so silent loss is visible.
- **Missing logs.** A log copy can fail (for example, refused under a restrictive permission mode). Mitigation: the report degrades to documents only and says why.
- **Permission prompts.** Running `python3` on a script outside the working directory may need approval in restrictive modes. Mitigation: the same "report and carry on" rule as for logs. The README gives the manual command.
- **Sensitive data.** The report contains everything in the transcripts, including file contents and command output. Mitigation: gitignore advice. Nothing is uploaded.
- **Large files.** A long execute run can produce several MB of transcripts, and so a large HTML file. Acceptable for a local file. Collapsed `<details>` keep it usable in a browser.

## Testing and validation

1. Unit tests: `python3 -m unittest discover tests`, using a small, hand-written fixture in `tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/` with a main transcript, one worker transcript and meta file, one errored tool call, one unknown record type, and one malformed line. The fixture holds no real session data.
2. Real data: copy the transcripts of a real poe session from `~/.claude/projects/` into a scratch `poe/<slug>/logs/` (outside the repository, never committed), run the script, and check the report in a browser against the acceptance criteria above. Check the token totals with a short independent `python3` sum over the same files.
3. End to end: `claude --plugin-dir .` in a scratch directory, run `/poe:poe report <slug>` on that scratch task, and confirm that `report.html` is written and the skill reports its path.
4. Static checks: `grep -nE '(src|href)="https?:' report.html` finds no external resources (escaped URLs inside log text don't match), and `claude plugin validate .` passes.

#9 · 2026-11-17T15:25:29.737Z · tool result · Bash
**Build a simple timeline per transcript.** Turn the useful records into a flat list of steps, each with: time, actor (orchestrator or worker name), kind, and text. The kinds are: - user promptshow the rest (2,420 more characters)
- assistant text
- thinking (collapsed)
- tool call (tool name and its input)
- tool result (output; marked as an error or denial as above)
- system or hook message

Each tool call is paired with its result by `tool_use_id`. An `Agent` tool call links to the worker's own timeline. Per transcript, and in total, the script adds up tokens, the number of tool calls and errors, the model or models, the duration (first to last timestamp), and the cost from the last `cost-state` record when present.

**Render.** Write one HTML file with inline CSS and no external resources, so it opens offline and can be attached or emailed as a single file. The layout follows Inspect's viewer:

- **Sidebar:** anchor links to the overview, the errors list, the task documents, and each session, with its workers indented under it.
- **Overview cards:** the task slug, the date generated, the status table from `status.md` as written, the number of sessions and workers, tool calls, errors, tokens, cost, duration, and the number of skipped records.
- **Errors:** every errored or denied tool call across all transcripts, each with its tool name, a one-line excerpt, and a link to the step. This is the stacktrace view.
- **Task documents:** `plan.md`, `status.md`, `summary.md`, phase briefs and progress notes, each in a collapsible `<details>` block of preformatted text.
- **Session timelines:** one section per session, ordered by first timestamp, then one per worker. Each step is a row with a coloured left border by kind, a timestamp, and its content. Tool inputs and outputs are in monospace inside `<details>`, with the first few lines visible. Error rows are red. A worker section starts with its type, description, model and totals, and links back to the step that started it.
- Light and dark colours through `prefers-color-scheme`. `<details>` handles collapsing, so the page needs no JavaScript.

All text from the logs goes through `html.escape`, because transcripts contain arbitrary file contents and command output. Content is never truncated in the HTML. Long blocks are collapsed, not cut, so the file stays complete for a stacktrace or a judge.

If `logs/` is missing or empty, the report still renders the documents and says that no transcripts were found.

The script prints the path of the written report and exits 0. It exits non-zero only if the task directory does not exist or the report cannot be written.

\"\"\"Flatten a tool_result's content, which is a string or a list of blocks.\"\"\"\n    if isinstance(content, str):\n        return content\n    if not isinstance(content, list):\n        return **Interface for phase 02** (plain dataclasses, standard library only; names may be adjusted if recorded in the progress notes):

- `Transcript`: `name` (session id or `agent-<id>`), `actor` (`orchestrator` or the worker's description/type), `agent_type`, `description`, `steps`, `models` (set), token totals (`input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens`, `cache_creation_tokens`), `tool_calls`, `errors`, `first_timestamp`, `last_timestamp`, `cost_usd` (or None), `skipped` (count), `parent_step` (the `Agent` call Step, for workers).
- `Session`: `session_id`, `main` (Transcript), `workers` (list of Transcript).
- `Task`: `slug`, `task_dir`, `documents` (ordered list of `(relative_path, text)` for `plan.md`, `status.md`, `summary.md`, `phases/*.md`, `progress/*.md`), `sessions` (ordered by first timestamp), `skipped` (total).
- `load_task(task_dir: Path) -> Task`.

**Rules from the plan.** Skip unknown record types and malformed lines, counting them. Pair calls and results by `tool_use_id`. Link workers via `.meta.json` `toolUseId` regardless of the tool's name (`Agent` or older `Task`). Errors: `is_error` true; denied: `permissionDecision.decision` present and not `accept`. Keep file order. Never truncate text.

**Check before trusting usage.** Claude Code may write one `assistant` record per content block, each repeating the same `message.id` and `message.usage`. Check this in the real transcripts. If it happens, count usage once per `message.id`, and record the rule in the progress notes (phase 03's independent sum must use the same rule).

Include: one `Agent` tool call linked to the worker, one tool result with `is_error: true`, one denied result, one tool result containing `<script>alert(1)</script>` (used by phase 02's escaping test), one unknown record type, one malformed line, and a `cost-state` record.

**Tests** (`unittest`): expected step count; each tool call paired with its result; error and denial flagged; worker linked via `toolUseId`; unknown type and malformed line skipped and counted; token totals; a task dir without `logs/` loads with no sessions.

# Plan: Inspect-style `report.html` for a poe task

## Objective

Turn what poe saves for a task in `poe/<task-slug>/` (plan, status, phase briefs, progress notes, summary, and the copied Claude Code transcripts in `logs/`) into one self-contained `poe/<task-slug>/report.html`. The page, styled after the Inspect AI log viewer, lets a person follow, step by step, what the orchestrator and each worker did: prompts, replies, tool calls and their results, errors, and token and cost totals.

The page has two later uses, which shape it now but are not built here:

- **Stacktrace:** when a run goes wrong, errored and denied tool calls can be found quickly and traced back to the step that caused them.
- **LLM-as-judge input:** all content is plain, server-rendered HTML text (no data hidden in JavaScript), so a model can be given the file as is.

## Scope

In scope:

- `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py` (new): a Python 3 script using only the standard library. `python3 poe_report.py poe/<task-slug>` reads that task directory and writes `poe/<task-slug>/report.html`.
- `tests/test_poe_report.py` and `tests/fixtures/` (new): `unittest` tests against a small, hand-written transcript fixture.
- `skills/poe/SKILL.md`:
  - a new `report [plan-path]` mode that (re)builds the report;
  - an automatic report build at the end of `execute` and `resume`, right after the log copy;
  - `report.html` added to the Files layout.
- `README.md`: the `report` mode, `report.html` in the layout, how to run the script by hand, and gitignore advice.

Out of scope:

- The LLM-as-judge itself, scoring, and any JSON or other machine-readable export.
- Converting to Inspect AI's `.eval` log format, or depending on `inspect-ai` or any other pip package. "Inspect style" means the look and layout, not the tool.
- A live server, auto-refresh, search boxes, charts, or comparing several tasks.
- Changing how logs are copied. The report works with whatever is in `logs/`.
- Rendering Markdown to HTML. The task's `.md` files are shown as preformatted text.
- The older draft at `plans/poe-report-dashboard/plan.md` (written before output moved to `poe/`). This file replaces it; it is left untouched for the user to delete.

## Assumptions

- The layout is the one in the current `SKILL.md`: `poe/<slug>/{plan.md, status.md, summary.md, phases/*.md, progress/*.md, logs/<session-id>/<session-id>.jsonl, logs/<session-id>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl, agent-<id>.meta.json}`. Any of these may be missing, and there may be several sessions (one per `plan`, `split`, `execute`, `resume` run).
- The Claude Code transcript format is not documented and changes between versions. Checked on this machine (Claude Code 2.1.326) against existing transcripts:
  - Each line is a JSON record with a `type`. The records that matter are `user` and `assistant` (with `message.content` blocks `text`, `thinking`, `tool_use`, `tool_result`), `system`, and `cost-state` (`totalCostUSD`, `totalDuration`, `modelUsage`). Other types (`attachment`, `api-request`, `api-request-blob`, `mode`, `session-owner`, `atis-latch`, `last-prompt`, `ai-title`, `permission-mode`, `tools-used`, `file-history-*`, `queue-operation`, ...) are internal and skipped.
  - `assistant` records carry `timestamp`, `message.model`, and `message.usage` (input, output, cache read and cache creation tokens).
  - `user` records holding a `tool_result` block may carry `toolUseResult` and `permissionDecision` (`decision`, `source`). The block itself may have `is_error: true`.
  - A worker's `agent-<id>.meta.json` holds `agentType`, `description` and `toolUseId`. `toolUseId` matches the `id` of the `Agent` `tool_use` block in the main transcript, which is how a worker is linked to the step that started it.
- A tool result counts as an error when `is_error` is true, or as denied when `permissionDecision.decision` is present and not `accept`. Both are read defensively.
- Records are shown in file order (the order Claude Code appended them). The `parentUuid` tree is not reconstructed.
- `python3` (3.9 or later) is available wherever poe runs. This machine has 3.12.
- Skills substitute `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}`, so `SKILL.md` can call the bundled script by its path. **To be confirmed during implementation** against the skills documentation; otherwise use `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`.

## Approach

### 1. The script, `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`

One file of small functions, in three steps.

**Load.** Read the task's `.md` files as text. For each `logs/<session-id>/` folder, parse the main `.jsonl` and each `subagents/agent-*.jsonl` with its `.meta.json`. A line that is not valid JSON, or a record without the expected fields, is counted and skipped; it never stops the script.

**Build a simple timeline per transcript.** Turn the useful records into a flat list of steps, each with: time, actor (orchestrator or worker name), kind, and text. The kinds are:

- user prompt
- assistant text
- thinking (collapsed)
- tool call (tool name and its input)
- tool result (output; marked as an error or denial as above)
- system or hook message

Each tool call is paired with its result by `tool_use_id`. An `Agent` tool call links to the worker's own timeline. Per transcript, and in total, the script adds up tokens, the number of tool calls and errors, the model or models, the duration (first to last timestamp), and the cost from the last `cost-state` record when present.

**Render.** Write one HTML file with inline CSS and no external resources, so it opens offline and can be attached or emailed as a single file. The layout follows Inspect's viewer:

- **Sidebar:** anchor links to the overview, the errors list, the task documents, and each session, with its workers indented under it.
- **Overview cards:** the task slug, the date generated, the status table from `status.md` as written, the number of sessions and workers, tool calls, errors, tokens, cost, duration, and the number of skipped records.
- **Errors:** every errored or denied tool call across all transcripts, each with its tool name, a one-line excerpt, and a link to the step. This is the stacktrace view.
- **Task documents:** `plan.md`, `status.md`, `summary.md`, phase briefs and progress notes, each in a collapsible `<details>` block of preformatted text.
- **Session timelines:** one section per session, ordered by first timestamp, then one per worker. Each step is a row with a coloured left border by kind, a timestamp, and its content. Tool inputs and outputs are in monospace inside `<details>`, with the first few lines visible. Error rows are red. A worker section starts with its type, description, model and totals, and links back to the step that started it.
- Light and dark colours through `prefers-color-scheme`. `<details>` handles collapsing, so the page needs no JavaScript.

All text from the logs goes through `html.escape`, because transcripts contain arbitrary file contents and command output. Content is never truncated in the HTML. Long blocks are collapsed, not cut, so the file stays complete for a stacktrace or a judge.

If `logs/` is missing or empty, the report still renders the documents and says that no transcripts were found.

The script prints the path of the written report and exits 0. It exits non-zero only if the task directory does not exist or the report cannot be written.

### 2. Skill changes, `skills/poe/SKILL.md`

- Operating modes gain `report [plan-path]`: copy the logs (as in the Logs section), then run `python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/poe_report.py" poe/<slug>` and give the user the path of the report.
- Execute mode step 9 and `resume`: after writing `summary.md` and copying the logs, build the report the same way. A failure is reported in the reply but never blocks the task, the same rule as for log copies.
- Files layout: add `report.html   step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/`.

### 3. README

Add the `report` mode to the usage list, `report.html` to the layout, the manual command `python3 <plugin>/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py poe/<slug>`, and a note that `report.html` contains the full transcripts, so it belongs in `.gitignore` alongside `logs/`.

## Acceptance criteria

- `python3 -m unittest discover tests` passes. The tests cover:
  - parsing the fixture: the expected number of steps, tool calls paired with their results, an error flagged, and a worker linked by `toolUseId`;
  - skipping unknown record types and malformed lines without crashing;
  - HTML escaping: a `<script>` string in a tool result appears escaped in the output;
  - a task directory without `logs/` still produces a report.
- Running the script on real transcripts (a poe session from this project, including one with workers, copied into a scratch `poe/<slug>/logs/`) writes a `report.html` that opens in a browser and shows:
  - all sessions and their workers;
  - every tool call, in order, with its result;
  - every `is_error` tool result, listed in the Errors section;
  - token totals that match a separate sum over `message.usage` in the same files.
- The report opens with no network access (no external URLs in the file).
- `SKILL.md` documents the `report` mode and the automatic build. `README.md` matches it. `claude plugin validate .` still passes.
- `/poe:poe report <slug>` run with `claude --plugin-dir .` in a scratch directory produces `poe/<slug>/report.html`.

## Risks

- **Transcript format changes.** The parser relies on undocumented fields. Mitigation: read fields defensively, skip what is unknown, and show the count of skipped records in the overview, so silent loss is visible.
- **Missing logs.** A log copy can fail (for example, refused under a restrictive permission mode). Mitigation: the report degrades to documents only and says why.
- **Permission prompts.** Running `python3` on a script outside the working directory may need approval in restrictive modes. Mitigation: the same "report and carry on" rule as for logs. The README gives the manual command.
- **Sensitive data.** The report contains everything in the transcripts, including file contents and command output. Mitigation: gitignore advice. Nothing is uploaded.
- **Large files.** A long execute run can produce several MB of transcripts, and so a large HTML file. Acceptable for a local file. Collapsed `<details>` keep it usable in a browser.

## Testing and validation

1. Unit tests: `python3 -m unittest discover tests`, using a small, hand-written fixture in `tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/` with a main transcript, one worker transcript and meta file, one errored tool call, one unknown record type, and one malformed line. The fixture holds no real session data.
2. Real data: copy the transcripts of a real poe session from `~/.claude/projects/` into a scratch `poe/<slug>/logs/` (outside the repository, never committed), run the script, and check the report in a browser against the acceptance criteria above. Check the token totals with a short independent `python3` sum over the same files.
3. End to end: `claude --plugin-dir .` in a scratch directory, run `/poe:poe report <slug>` on that scratch task, and confirm that `report.html` is written and the skill reports its path.
4. Static checks: `grep -nE '(src|href)="https?:' report.html` finds no external resources (escaped URLs inside log text don't match), and `claude plugin validate .` passes.

    13	
    14	## Operating modes
    15	
    16	- `plan <task>` (or a task description without a mode): write `plan.md` only, then stop so the user can review it. Do not split it into phases and do not edit implementation files.
    17	- `split [plan-path]`: split a reviewed `plan.md` into phases and work units, then stop for user approval. Do not edit implementation files.
    18	- `execute [plan-path]`: execute a plan that has been split and approved. If the plan has no phase briefs yet, say so and ask the user to run `split` first.
    19	- When a mode is given no path, use the unambiguous active plan in `poe/`; otherwise ask for the path.
    20	- `resume [plan-path]`: read `status.md` and the progress notes, compare them with the actual files, tests, and git diff, reconcile discrepancies, and continue only incomplete approved work. When it finishes or stops, write `summary.md` as in Execute mode step 9.
    21	- For small, single-file or tightly coupled changes, recommend doing the work directly instead of forcing subagents. If the user still requests orchestration, comply.
    22	
    23	## Files
    24	
    25	Everything for one task lives in `poe/<short-task-slug>/`, relative to the current working directory (the directory the session was started in, even if that is not the repository root). Create `poe/` and the task directory if they do not exist (`mkdir -p`). Never overwrite the directory of a different task; pick a distinct slug.
    26	
    27	```
    28	poe/<short-task-slug>/
    29	  plan.md              what will be built and why (written by `plan`)
    30	  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units (written by `split`)
    31	  status.md            state of every phase and unit, and the next action
    32	  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker during execution
    33	  summary.md           execution summary, overwritten at the end of each `execute`/`resume` run
    34	  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts of each session that worked on this task
    35	    <session-id>.jsonl         main (orchestrator) transcript
    36	    subagents/agent-*.jsonl    worker transcripts
    37	    subagents/agent-*.meta.json
    38	```
    39	
    40	## Plan mode: write `plan.md`
    41	
    42	1. Inspect only the relevant repository context, instructions, tests, and environment. Use read-only exploration subagents when exploration would otherwise overwhelm parent context.
    43	2. Write `poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md` from what the user wants to do: objective, scope and exclusions, assumptions, the approach, acceptance criteria, risks, and how the result will be tested or validated. This file describes the work itself; it does not mention phases, subagents, or token budgets.
    44	3. **Do not overengineer.** Plan the simplest approach that meets the stated requirements:
    45	   - Build only what the user asked for. No speculative features, configuration options, plugin systems, or abstraction layers added "for later".
    46	   - Prefer the tools, libraries, and patterns the repository already uses over introducing new ones.
    47	   - The resulting code must stay readable by humans: plain control flow, descriptive names, small functions, and comments only where the reason is not obvious. Prefer a few clear lines over a clever one.
    48	   - Keep the process as light as the code. Do not add steps, files, or reviews that the task does not need.
    49	4. Do not silently choose a major architecture if it needs user approval; ask before writing it into the plan.

    51

    53

    68	4. The worker implements or analyzes only its assigned unit and runs the relevant tests. It writes its progress notes **as it works**, not only at the end: it updates the notes file after each meaningful step, so that the notes are current if the worker stops, is compacted, or fails.
    69	5. Run independent, non-overlapping units in parallel only when safe. Never silently overwrite another agent's edits.
    70	6. Check the worker's claims against the actual files, test outputs, and git diff. For significant or high-risk changes, use a separate fresh-context reviewer before marking the unit verified. Fix or reassign failed units with a new scoped brief.
    71	7. Update `status.md` after every unit. Keep the parent response concise: phase, tests, blockers, and next action. Reference files rather than returning large transcripts.
    72	8. Move to the next approved phase after verification. Stop for genuinely ambiguous requirements, security-sensitive changes, destructive operations, deployments, or decisions beyond the approved plan.
    73	9. At completion, run integration tests, review the full change set, and report what passed, what remains uncertain, and where the files were saved. Write the same report to `summary.md` (what passed, what remains uncertain, blockers, and file locations), overwriting any earlier one; `status.md` stays the source of truth for state.
    74	
    75	## Logs
    76	
    77	At the end of every mode (`plan`, `split`, `execute`, `resume`), and whenever execution stops for user input, copy this session's transcripts into the task directory:
    78	
    79	```sh
    80	dest="poe/<short-task-slug>/logs/${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}"
    81	src=$(find "${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects" -name "${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}.jsonl" | head -1)
    82	mkdir -p "$dest"
    83	cp "$src" "$dest/"
    84	[ -d "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" ] && cp -R "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" "$dest/"
    85	```
    86	
    87	- Re-running it overwrites the earlier copy with the current, longer transcript, so repeating it is safe.
    88	- The copy is a snapshot: anything after it (such as the final reply of the turn) is missing until the next copy.
    89	- If the transcript cannot be found (`src` is empty or `cp` fails), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing log never blocks the task.
    90	
    91	## Progress notes
    92	
    93	Each worker keeps one file, `progress/NN-unit.md`, and updates it throughout its run. `resume` and any follow-on worker rely on it, so it must make sense to an agent that has seen nothing else.
    94	
    95	```
    96	# Unit ID: status (in progress | done | partial | blocked)
    97	## Done so far
    98	## Files changed or produced
    99	## Tests run and observed results
   100	## Decisions and assumptions
   101	## Next step / remaining work
   102	```
   103	
   104	When a unit is handed to a second worker, that worker reads the existing notes and continues in the same file.
   105	
   106	## Context policy: about 100k tokens per agent
   107	
   108	- 100k is a **soft target**, not a hard limit. Never claim to know exact token usage unless a tool reports it; otherwise judge by how much has been read and produced.
   109	- Hand over at a natural boundary, not at a number. When a worker nears the target, it finishes the step it is in the middle of (an edit in flight, a test run, a debugging thread that is about to resolve), then brings its progress notes up to date and returns `partial`. Going moderately over the target to finish a coherent step is correct; stopping mid-edit to respect the number is not.
   110	- The orchestrator then starts a **fresh non-fork worker** for the remainder, pointed at the progress notes. Do not keep resuming a bloated agent.
   111	- Prefer this file-based handover to automatic compaction. Automatic compaction is a backstop: it can fire in the middle of a step and loses detail, which is why the notes are kept current throughout.
   112	- Keep the parent lean: orchestration and short summaries only; delegate large searches, code reading, and log interpretation.
   113	- Avoid unnecessary parallelism, repeated repository scans, duplicate reading, and verbose inter-agent messages.
   114	
   115	## Constraints
   116	
   117	- Follow the project's `CLAUDE.md`, security practices, and user instructions.
   118	- Implement what the plan says and no more. Keep code simple and readable by humans; do not add abstractions or features the plan does not call for.
   119	- Preserve existing user changes; check git status before edits. Do not commit, push, merge, delete, or deploy without authorization.
   120	- Never infer that a test passed without observing its result. Preserve unknowns rather than fabricating evidence.
   121	- If an agent has no delegation tool, say so and execute sequentially using the same file-based phase structure.

2. Size each unit so that one subagent can finish it in roughly 100k tokens of context, counting the files it must read, the edits, and the test output. Split a unit that looks larger; merge units that are trivially small. Use as few phases and units as the work actually needs.

## Execute mode

1. Read `plan.md`, `status.md`, and the brief of the next phase. Verify that the plan is approved and the prerequisites of the next unit are satisfied.
2. Dispatch a **fresh non-fork subagent** for each work unit. Prefer the `poe:phase-worker` subagent bundled with this plugin (if it is not available under that name, look for `phase-worker`); otherwise use an appropriate built-in agent capable of doing the work. Do not use a fork for routine delegation: it inherits the parent's history.
3. Give the worker only: the goal, the phase-brief path, necessary repository paths, interfaces and dependencies, allowed files, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. Do not paste the parent conversation or large tool output.
=====README
     1	# poe: phase orchestrator for Claude Code
     2	
     3	A Claude Code plugin with one skill (`poe`) and one subagent (`phase-worker`). It plans a task, splits it into work units sized for about 100k tokens each, and runs each unit in a fresh-context subagent that keeps file-based progress notes.
     4	
     5	Requires Claude Code v2.1.296 or later.
     6	
     7	## Installation
     8	
     9	This repository is both the plugin and its own marketplace. Add the marketplace, then install the plugin:
    10	
    11	```sh
    12	claude plugin marketplace add sreejancodes/poe
    13	claude plugin install poe@poe-tools
    14	```
    15	
    16	The same commands work inside a session as `/plugin marketplace add sreejancodes/poe` and `/plugin install poe@poe-tools`. Then run `/reload-plugins` or start a new session.
    17	
    18	By default the plugin is installed at **user** scope, so it is available in every project. Add `--scope project` to enable it only for the current repository, recorded in `.claude/settings.json`.
    19	
    20	### Update and uninstall
    21	
    22	Third-party marketplaces do not update automatically. To get the latest version:
    23	
    24	```sh
    25	claude plugin marketplace update poe-tools
    26	claude plugin update poe@poe-tools
    27	```
    28	
    29	To remove it:
    30	
    31	```sh
    32	claude plugin uninstall poe@poe-tools
    33	claude plugin marketplace remove poe-tools
    34	```
    35	
    36	### Offering it to everyone on a project
    37	
    38	Commit this to the project's `.claude/settings.json`:
    39	
    40	```json
    41	{
    42	  "extraKnownMarketplaces": {
    43	    "poe-tools": { "source": { "source": "github", "repo": "sreejancodes/poe" } }
    44	  },
    45	  "enabledPlugins": { "poe@poe-tools": true }
    46	}
    47	```
    48	
    49	Once a collaborator trusts the folder, the marketplace is registered for them. They still need to run `claude plugin install poe@poe-tools --scope project` once.
    50	
    51	## Usage
    52	
    53	Plugin skills are namespaced, so the command is `/poe:poe`:
    54	
    55	```text
    56	/poe:poe plan Build an HPV ORF analysis pipeline with tests and reproducible outputs
    57	/poe:poe split poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
    58	/poe:poe execute poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
    59	/poe:poe resume poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
    60	```
    61	
    62	- **Plan** writes `plan.md` from what you asked for, keeping the approach simple and the code human-readable, then stops. Review the file, edit it or ask for changes, and repeat until you are happy with it.
    63	- **Split** takes the reviewed `plan.md` and splits it into phases and work units, each sized for one subagent at roughly 100k tokens. It then stops for approval.
    64	- **Execute** hands each work unit to a fresh `poe:phase-worker` subagent, which writes progress notes as it works. The orchestrator verifies each unit before moving on.
    65	- **Resume** uses the status file and progress notes to continue safely.
    66	
    67	## Files
    68	
    69	Everything for one task is written under `poe/<task-slug>/` in the directory where Claude Code was started. `poe/` is created if it does not exist.
    70	
    71	```
    72	poe/<task-slug>/
    73	  plan.md              what will be built and why
    74	  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units
    75	  status.md            state of every phase and unit
    76	  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker
    77	  summary.md           execution summary, written at the end of execute/resume
    78	  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts (.jsonl) of each session, including worker subagents
    79	```
    80	
    81	The copied logs contain the full conversation, including file contents and command output. Normally add `poe/*/logs/` (or all of `poe/`) to `.gitignore`.
    82	
    83	Tasks created by older versions in `plans/` can be moved with `mv plans/<slug> poe/<slug>`.
    84	
    85	## Context settings
    86	
    87	The 100k figure is a soft target, not a hard cap. A worker nearing it finishes the step it is in the middle of, updates its progress notes, and returns. The orchestrator then starts a fresh worker from those notes.
    88	
    89	`phase-worker` sets `autoCompactWindow: 150000`. This is a backstop above the target, so that automatic compaction does not interrupt a step at exactly 100k. It does not cap cumulative usage. Older Claude Code versions ignore the setting without warning.
    90	
    91	The `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` environment variable takes precedence over a subagent's `autoCompactWindow`. Setting it to 100000 would make workers compact at 100k regardless, so leave it unset or set it above the target.
    92	
    93	## Repository layout
    94	
    95	```
    96	.claude-plugin/plugin.json        plugin manifest
    97	.claude-plugin/marketplace.json   marketplace listing this repo as the plugin source
    98	skills/poe/SKILL.md               the /poe:poe orchestrator skill
    99	agents/phase-worker.md            the worker subagent
   100	```
   101	
   102	To try local changes without installing: `claude --plugin-dir /path/to/this/repo`.
   103	
   104	## References
   105	
   106	- https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugins
   107	- https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills
   108	- https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents

 - **Plan** writes `plan.md` from what you asked for, keeping the approach simple and the code human-readable, then stops. Review the file, edit it or ask for changes, and repeat until you are happy with it.
 - **Split** takes the reviewed `plan.md` and splits it into phases and work units, each sized for one subagent at roughly 100k tokens. It then stops for approval.
 - **Execute** hands each work unit to a fresh `poe:phase-worker` subagent, which writes progress notes as it works. The orchestrator verifies each unit before moving on.
 - **Resume** uses the status file and progress notes to continue safely.
+- **Report** copies the session's logs and (re)builds `report.html`, a step-by-step view of what the orchestrator and each worker did. Execute and resume also build it automatically at the end, after `summary.md` and the log copy; if the build fails, the reply says so and the task carries on.
