# Extend Claude with skills

> Create, manage, and share skills to extend Claude's capabilities in Claude Code. Includes custom commands and bundled skills.

Skills extend what Claude can do. Create a `SKILL.md` file with instructions, and Claude adds it to its toolkit. Claude uses skills when relevant, or you can invoke one directly with `/skill-name`.

Create a skill when you keep pasting the same instructions, checklist, or multi-step procedure into chat, or when a section of CLAUDE.md has grown into a procedure rather than a fact. Unlike CLAUDE.md content, a skill's body loads only when it's used, so long reference material costs almost nothing until you need it.

> [!NOTE]
>
> For built-in commands like `/help` and `/compact`, and bundled skills like `/debug` and `/code-review`, see the [commands reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/commands.md).
>
> **Custom commands have been merged into skills.** Your existing `.claude/commands/` files keep working. Claude can invoke them without you typing the command. Edit a command to stop Claude from invoking it on its own, or convert a command that needs more than one file to a skill:
>
> * **Stop Claude from invoking a command on its own**: add [`disable-model-invocation: true`](#control-who-invokes-a-skill) to the command file's [frontmatter](#frontmatter-reference)
> * **Add [supporting files](#add-supporting-files) such as scripts**: convert the command to a skill. For example, move `.claude/commands/deploy.md` to `.claude/skills/deploy/SKILL.md`. The [folder name becomes the command](#how-a-skill-gets-its-command-name), so you still type `/deploy`.

Claude Code skills follow the [Agent Skills](https://agentskills.io) open standard, which works across multiple AI tools. Claude Code extends the standard with additional features like [invocation control](#control-who-invokes-a-skill), [subagent execution](#run-skills-in-a-subagent), and [dynamic context injection](#inject-dynamic-context). See [Using skill frontmatter outside Claude Code](#using-skill-frontmatter-outside-claude-code) for which frontmatter fields are part of the standard and which are Claude Code extensions.

## Bundled skills

Claude Code includes a set of bundled skills, such as `/doctor`, `/code-review`, `/batch`, `/debug`, `/loop`, and `/claude-api`. Bundled skills are prompt-based: they give Claude detailed instructions and let it orchestrate the work using its tools. Most built-in commands instead execute fixed logic directly.

You invoke a bundled skill by typing `/` followed by the skill name. Claude invokes some bundled skills automatically when relevant.

Most bundled skills are available in every session. A few depend on a specific feature: `/workflow-authoring`, for example, is available only when [dynamic workflows](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/workflows.md) are enabled.

To turn bundled skills off, use the [`disableBundledSkills`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings-reference#disablebundledskills) setting.

> [!NOTE]
>
> The [`/doctor`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/commands#all-commands) setup checkup stays typable when `disableBundledSkills` is on. To hide it, set the `DISABLE_DOCTOR_COMMAND` environment variable or a [`skillOverrides`](#override-skill-visibility-from-settings) entry of `"doctor": "off"`.

Bundled skills are listed alongside built-in commands in the [commands reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/commands.md), marked **Skill** in the Purpose column.

### Check your setup with `/doctor`

Run `/doctor` at the Claude Code prompt for a setup checkup that diagnoses issues and can fix them. Claude reports its findings first and asks for confirmation before changing anything. The checkup covers these areas:

* **Installation health**: duplicate or leftover installs, `PATH` problems, unparseable settings files, and whether a newer version is available on your [release channel](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/setup.md#configure-release-channel)
* **Extensions**: unused skills, MCP servers, and plugins compared with their context cost, and slow [hooks](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md)
* **`CLAUDE.md` files**: local `CLAUDE.md` files that duplicate checked-in ones, checked-in [`CLAUDE.md` content Claude could derive from the codebase](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory#my-claude-md-is-too-large), and the always-loaded guidance that remains, which Claude offers to migrate into skills and nested `CLAUDE.md` files that load on demand
* **Permissions**: an offer to make [auto mode](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions.md#permission-modes) your default permission mode and to [pre-approve](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions.md) read-only commands that you frequently deny
* **Usage**: on a Pro, Max, Ultra, Team, or Enterprise plan, the [changes `/usage` recommends](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/costs#plan-usage-breakdown) to lower your plan usage, measured over the last 7 days. Claude proposes the changes it can make itself and reports the rest as advice, such as a command for you to run. The usage check requires Claude Code v2.1.305 or later, and `/doctor` leaves it out while [Remote Control](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/remote-control.md) is connected.

For read-only installation diagnostics without starting a session, run `claude doctor` in your terminal instead.

To audit your instructions rather than your setup, run `/doctor prompt-audit` at the Claude Code prompt. Claude [checks your `CLAUDE.md` files, skills, and other configuration](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory#audit-your-instruction-files) for outdated or conflicting instructions instead of running the checkup. The `prompt-audit` subcommand requires Claude Code v2.1.283 or later.

### Run and verify your app

Three bundled skills work together to launch your app and confirm changes against the running app instead of tests alone:

| Skill | Purpose |
| :- | :- |
| `/run` | Launch and drive your app to see a change working |
| `/verify` | Build and run your app to confirm a code change does what it should, without falling back to tests or type checks |
| `/run-skill-generator` | Teach `/run` and `/verify` how to build and launch your project |

`/run` and `/verify` work without setup. They infer the launch from your project type (CLI, server, TUI, browser-driven) and from what's in your README, `package.json`, or `Makefile`. That inference gets unreliable for projects that need anything beyond a standard launch: a database, an env file, a graphical session, a multi-step build.

`/run-skill-generator` records the recipe instead. It gets your app running from a clean environment, captures what worked (the install commands, the env vars, the launch script), and commits it as a per-project skill at `.claude/skills/run-<name>/`. After that, `/run`, `/verify`, and any other agent in the repo follow the recorded recipe instead of rediscovering it. Run `/run-skill-generator` once per project, and again if the build or launch process changes.

`/verify` can also record its own recipe. When it has to build and drive your app without a recorded recipe, it writes what worked to `.claude/skills/verify/SKILL.md` at the repo root, or in the touched package directory in a monorepo, so later runs and other agents follow the same steps. At the repo root, the recorded skill replaces the bundled `/verify`.

Claude edits the recorded file only when it steered a run wrong, such as a command that failed or a missing step, so you can commit the file without per-session diffs.

### Run your checks before each commit

When a session starts with a skill named `verify` or `simplify` in place, Claude Code's commit instructions tell Claude to run it right before each commit, except for changes to docs or tests. This requires Claude Code v2.1.286 or later. Claude gets that instruction when these conditions hold at the start of the session:

* **Location**: the skill loads from the enterprise, personal, project, or additional-directory [location](#where-skills-live), or from a `.claude/commands/` file with that name. The recipe that `/verify` records at your repo root is a project skill, so it counts. The bundled `/verify` and `/simplify`, plugin skills, and skills from your claude.ai account don't count.
* **Invocation**: Claude can invoke the skill. If you've [stopped Claude from invoking it](#control-who-invokes-a-skill), for example with `disable-model-invocation: true`, Claude doesn't get the instruction.
* **Git instructions**: you haven't turned off [`includeGitInstructions`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings-reference#includegitinstructions). Turning it off removes this instruction together with the rest of the built-in commit and PR instructions.

### Work on Claude API projects

The bundled `/claude-api` skill loads [Claude API](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/overview) and [Managed Agents](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/managed-agents/overview) reference material for your project's language. Claude also activates it automatically when your code imports `anthropic` or `@anthropic-ai/sdk`.

To start one of the skill's workflows, type a subcommand after the skill name at the Claude Code prompt, for example `/claude-api migrate`. The table lists what each subcommand does and the earliest Claude Code version that includes it. `migrate` and `managed-agents-onboard` predate v2.1.221, the oldest version the table tracks.

| Subcommand | What it does | Minimum version |
| :- | :- | :- |
| `migrate` | Update your existing Claude API code to a newer model | Earlier than v2.1.221 |
| `upgrade` | Upgrade your project's Anthropic SDK to a new major version and update your code for its breaking changes | v2.1.236 or later |
| `api-version` | Update your Claude API code for a newer [API version](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/versioning), then switch your requests to that version | v2.1.307 or later |
| `managed-agents-onboard` | Walk through creating a new Managed Agent | Earlier than v2.1.221 |
| `managed-agents-onboard <url>` | Build the Managed Agent that the page at the URL describes, such as a page in the [Managed Agents docs](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/managed-agents/overview) | v2.1.290 or later |
| `managed-agents-onboard <quickstart-name>` | Build one of the Console's quickstart templates, such as `deep-researcher`. If you give one word that isn't a template name, Claude lists the valid names | v2.1.290 or later |
| `managed-agents-secure [<agent-id>]` | Read the settings of one Managed Agent and report where they match the [Secure your Managed Agents deployment](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/managed-agents/secure-deployment) guide or differ from it. Requires the [`ant` CLI](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/cli-sdks-libraries/cli/quickstart) | v2.1.304 or later |
| `prompt-audit` | Flag instructions written for older models in your prompts, skills, and tool descriptions and propose fixes as a diff | v2.1.221 or later |
| `cost-optimize` | Profile where your project's Claude API spend goes and propose savings from options such as prompt caching, trimming unneeded input and output tokens, batch processing, effort, and model choice, one change at a time | v2.1.247 or later |
| `latency-optimize` | Find where the time goes in your existing Claude API code and propose changes that reduce how long people wait | v2.1.319 or later |
| `build-eval` | Build an eval set for your Claude-powered app | v2.1.259 or later |
| `hillclimb` | Iteratively improve your app against an existing eval | v2.1.259 or later |
| `preserved-thinking-migration` | Find the edits your integration makes to earlier turns, its system prompt, or its tool list that invalidate [preserved thinking](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/preserved-thinking) blocks, measure how much reasoning each one drops, and propose fixes one at a time, re-measuring after each change | v2.1.282 or later |

## Getting started

### Create your first skill

This example creates a skill that summarizes the uncommitted changes in your git repository and flags anything risky. It pulls the live diff into the prompt before Claude reads it, so the response is grounded in your actual working tree rather than what Claude can guess from open files. Claude loads the skill automatically when you ask about your changes, or you can invoke it directly with `/summarize-changes`.

**1. Create the skill directory**

Create a directory for the skill in your personal skills folder. Personal skills are available across all your projects.

```bash theme={null}
mkdir -p ~/.claude/skills/summarize-changes
```

**2. Write SKILL.md**

Every skill needs a `SKILL.md` file with two parts: YAML frontmatter between `---` markers that tells Claude when to use the skill, and markdown content with the instructions Claude follows when the skill runs. The directory name, or the frontmatter `name` when you set one, becomes the command you type, and the `description` helps Claude decide when to load the skill automatically.

Save this to `~/.claude/skills/summarize-changes/SKILL.md`:

```yaml theme={null}
---
description: Summarizes uncommitted changes and flags anything risky. Use when the user asks what changed, wants a commit message, or asks to review their diff.
---

## Current changes

!`git diff HEAD`

## Instructions

Summarize the changes above in two or three bullet points, then list any risks you notice such as missing error handling, hardcoded values, or tests that need updating. If the diff is empty, say there are no uncommitted changes.
```

The `` !`git diff HEAD` `` line uses [dynamic context injection](#inject-dynamic-context): Claude Code runs the command and replaces the line with its output before Claude sees the skill content, so the instructions arrive with the current diff already inlined.

**3. Test the skill**

Open a git project, make a small edit to any file, and start Claude Code by running `claude`. You can test the skill two ways.

**Let Claude invoke it automatically** by asking something that matches the description:

```text theme={null}
What did I change?
```

**Or invoke it directly** with the skill name:

```text theme={null}
/summarize-changes
```

Either way, Claude should respond with a short summary of your edit and a list of risks.

## Choose where skills load

Where you save a skill decides which sessions load it. Save it under your home directory to get it in every project, commit it to a repository to share it with everyone who works there, or distribute it through a plugin or managed settings to reach a whole team.

| Location | Path | Loads in |
| :- | :- | :- |
| Enterprise | `.claude/skills/<skill-name>/SKILL.md` in the [managed settings directory](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/managed-settings.md#delivery-mechanisms) | All users on machines where your organization deploys it |
| Personal | `~/.claude/skills/<skill-name>/SKILL.md` | All your projects on this machine, but not [Cowork or cloud sessions](#skills-in-cowork-and-cloud-sessions) |
| Project | `.claude/skills/<skill-name>/SKILL.md` | Sessions in this repository, but not [cloud threads in a project](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-projects.md#what-threads-pick-up-from-your-repositories). Commit it so your team gets it too |
| Nested | `<subdir>/.claude/skills/<skill-name>/SKILL.md` | Sessions started in or below `<subdir>`, but not cloud threads in a project. Outside those threads, a session started above it loads the skill once Claude works on files there. See [monorepos and subdirectories](#discovery-from-parent-and-nested-directories) |
| Additional directory | `.claude/skills/<skill-name>/SKILL.md` in a directory you pass with `--add-dir` | That session. See [directories outside the project](#skills-from-additional-directories) |
| Plugin | `<plugin>/skills/<skill-name>/SKILL.md` | Wherever the [plugin](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugins/overview.md) is enabled, as `/plugin-name:skill-name` |
| claude.ai account | Skills enabled for your claude.ai account | Cowork sessions, cloud sessions, and terminal sessions where you sign in with that account. See [Skills synced from claude.ai](#how-synced-skills-behave) |

Skill folders also follow these rules:

* **Symlinked folders**: a `<skill-name>` entry in the enterprise, personal, or project location can be a symlink to a directory elsewhere on disk. Claude Code reads `SKILL.md` from the target and loads the skill once even if several locations point at the same target. Plugin skills [handle symlinks differently](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugins/host-marketplace.md#share-files-within-a-marketplace-with-symlinks).
* **Reserved name `synced`**: don't name a skill folder `synced`, in any capitalization. Claude Code uses `~/.claude/skills/synced/` for [skills downloaded from claude.ai](#where-synced-skills-load) and skips a skill you author at that name in the enterprise, personal, and project locations.
* **Reserved name `anthropic-skills`**: outside a plugin, a skill folder or command file whose name is `anthropic-skills` or starts with `anthropic-skills:` doesn't load. See [Names reserved for synced skills](#names-reserved-for-synced-skills).
* **Characters in the name**: outside a plugin, a skill folder doesn't load when its name has a character such as a zero-width space or `<`. See [Characters a skill name can't hold](#characters-a-skill-name-cant-hold).
* **Command files**: a Markdown file in `.claude/commands/` is the older format and still works. It supports the same [frontmatter](#frontmatter-reference) except `name` and `paths`. To find the name you type to invoke it, see [How a skill gets its command name](#how-a-skill-gets-its-command-name). Prefer a skill for new work, since skills also support [supporting files](#add-supporting-files).
* **Skill folder as a plugin**: add a `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` to a personal or project skill folder and Claude Code can load it as a [plugin](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugins/loading.md#plugins-shared-through-a-repository) named `<name>@skills-dir` with its own agents, hooks, and MCP servers. In a project's `.claude/skills/`, this requires accepting the workspace trust dialog first, and in either location it requires that your administrator hasn't [blocked skills-directory plugins](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugins/loading.md#skills-directory-plugins-blocked-by-managed-settings).

### Load skills in monorepos and subdirectories

Claude Code loads project skills from `.claude/skills/` in the directory where you start it and in every parent directory up to the repository root, so starting in `packages/frontend/` still picks up skills defined at the root. When you [move the session with `/cd`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions#move-the-session-to-another-directory) on v2.1.246 or later, Claude Code adds the new directory's project skills.

In a session running in a linked [git worktree](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/worktrees.md) that you created with `--worktree` or `git worktree add`, Claude Code searches parent directories only up to the worktree root. On Claude Code v2.1.277 or later, when the worktree checkout has no `.claude/skills` directory at its root, Claude Code loads the main checkout's project skills instead. See [What worktrees share with the main checkout](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/worktrees.md#what-worktrees-share-with-the-main-checkout).

Skills in a `.claude/skills/` directory below where you started don't load at startup. They load the first time Claude reads or edits a file in that subdirectory and stay available for the rest of the session. Until then they don't appear in the `/` menu and you can't invoke them by name. To load them sooner, run `/add-dir` with the subdirectory's path, which requires Claude Code v2.1.257 or later. For a worktree session that you start from the desktop app, see [What worktrees share with the main checkout](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/worktrees.md#what-worktrees-share-with-the-main-checkout).

When a nested skill's directory name matches another skill's name, both stay available. With a `deploy` skill at the repository root and another in `apps/web/.claude/skills/`:

* `/deploy` runs the root skill. Claude Code also lists the directory-qualified variants for Claude, with an instruction to invoke the one whose directory holds the files it's working on, so the nested skill still applies to work in `apps/web/`.
* `/apps/web:deploy` runs the nested skill on its own. Its description names the directory it applies to.

### Load skills from a directory outside the project

When you add a directory with `--add-dir` or `/add-dir`, Claude Code loads the skills in that directory's `.claude/skills/`, along with its `.claude/commands/` and `.claude/agents/`. Directories the Agent SDK adds through [`additionalDirectories`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#options) in TypeScript or [`add_dirs`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#claudeagentoptions) in Python load the same way, because the SDK passes them as `--add-dir`. The `permissions.additionalDirectories` setting in `settings.json` grants file access only and loads none of these.

Claude Code watches `.claude/skills/` in a directory you pass with `--add-dir` at launch, as [Edit a skill during a session](#live-change-detection) describes. It doesn't watch the added directory's `.claude/commands/` or `.claude/agents/`, so restart the session after changing a file there.

These loads depend on the `project` [setting source](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/claude-code-features.md#control-filesystem-settings-with-settingsources), which is on by default. A [`strictPluginOnlyCustomization`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings-reference#strictpluginonlycustomization) policy, [bare mode](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/headless.md#start-faster-with-bare-mode), and [`--safe-mode`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference#cli-flags) each restrict them further, as those pages describe. See [Additional directories grant file access, not configuration](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions.md#additional-directories-grant-file-access-not-configuration) for the full table of what an added directory loads, including `CLAUDE.md` and plugin settings.

#### Run a same-named skill from an added directory

An added directory's skill gets a prefix when its folder name clashes with an enterprise, personal, or project skill, or one in a directory added earlier. To run it, type `/<prefix>:<skill-name>` at the Claude Code prompt. The `/` menu lists the skill under its prefixed name, and [How a skill gets its command name](#how-a-skill-gets-its-command-name) describes where the prefix comes from. That name requires Claude Code v2.1.309 or later.

For example, with `--add-dir ../cli` and a `verify` skill in both your project and `../cli`:

* `/verify` runs your project's skill
* `/cli:verify` runs the skill from `../cli`

### Resolve skills that share a name

When two skills share a directory or file name, where each one came from decides which one `/name` runs. For a name set by the frontmatter `name` field, see [How a skill gets its command name](#how-a-skill-gets-its-command-name).

| Same name in | Which one runs |
| :- | :- |
| Two of enterprise, personal, and project | Enterprise over personal, and personal over project. With `deploy` in both `~/.claude/skills/` and the project's `.claude/skills/`, `/deploy` runs the personal one |
| Any of those locations and a [bundled skill](#bundled-skills) | Your skill replaces the bundled command, but not its aliases. A project `code-review` skill replaces `/code-review`, and the bundled alias `/review` never runs your skill |
| Any of those locations and a [built-in command](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/commands.md) | In a local terminal session, your skill replaces the built-in command, but not its aliases. A project `usage` skill replaces `/usage`, and the built-in alias `/cost` still runs the built-in command |
| A skill and a file in `.claude/commands/` | The skill |
| A project-root skill and a nested skill | Both load. See [monorepos and subdirectories](#discovery-from-parent-and-nested-directories) |
| An enterprise, personal, or project skill and a skill in an [added directory outside the project](#skills-from-additional-directories) | The enterprise, personal, or project skill, while the added directory's skill runs under a prefixed name. See [run a same-named skill from an added directory](#run-a-same-named-skill-from-an-added-directory) |
| Two [added directories](#skills-from-additional-directories) outside the project | The skill from the directory added first, in `--add-dir` or `/add-dir` order. The other runs under a [prefixed name](#run-a-same-named-skill-from-an-added-directory) |
| A plugin skill and a skill at any of the locations above | Both load, because plugin skills are namespaced as `/plugin-name:skill-name` |
| Any of the above and the short name of a skill [synced from your claude.ai account](#how-synced-skills-behave) | The other skill or command. The synced skill is then listed and runs only under its full name. See [When a synced skill name matches another command](#when-a-synced-skill-name-matches-another-command) |

### Use skills in Cowork and cloud sessions

[Cowork](https://claude.com/product/cowork) sessions and [cloud sessions](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cloud-environments.md#what-carries-over-from-your-setup), including [cloud scheduled tasks](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/routines.md), don't read `~/.claude/skills/` on your machine. Both interactive and scheduled Cowork sessions load the skills enabled for your claude.ai account, synced at session start. Manage those skills at [claude.ai/customize/skills/yours](https://claude.ai/customize/skills/yours).

If a skill exists only in `~/.claude/skills/` on your machine, Claude Code reports that the skill was not found when a [cloud scheduled task](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/routines.md) invokes it. This happens because each run starts as a fresh cloud session. To make a personal skill available in these sessions:

* For Cowork sessions, cloud sessions, and cloud threads in a [project](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-projects.md#what-threads-pick-up-from-your-repositories), enable the skill for your claude.ai account. In a project's cloud thread, skills committed to a repository's `.claude/skills/` aren't available. [Some sessions in a self-hosted environment](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/self-hosted-environments-configuration.md#how-each-session’s-config-is-assembled) don't load your account's skills.
* For cloud sessions other than project threads, you can instead commit the skill to the repository's `.claude/skills/`. Plugins declared in the repository's `.claude/settings.json` and plugins enabled only in your user settings [don't load in cloud sessions](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cloud-environments.md#what-carries-over-from-your-setup).

[Desktop scheduled tasks](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/desktop-scheduled-tasks.md) run locally on your machine, so they do load `~/.claude/skills/`.

### Skills synced from claude.ai

This section applies to you if you use Cowork or cloud sessions, or sign in to Claude Code in your terminal with a claude.ai account. In those sessions, Claude Code loads the skills enabled for your claude.ai account, with no setup on your part, as [Where synced skills load](#where-synced-skills-load) describes. Those skills include the ones you create or turn on in your claude.ai settings, skills your organization provides there, and Anthropic's built-in skills such as `pdf` and `xlsx`.

Claude Code downloads a synced skill from your account rather than reading a file you wrote on the machine where the session runs, so it applies rules to synced skills that don't apply to the skills you store in the [skills locations](#where-skills-live).

#### Where synced skills load

In a Cowork or cloud session, Claude Code loads the skills enabled for your claude.ai account, and [Skills in Cowork and cloud sessions](#skills-in-cowork-and-cloud-sessions) says how to choose which skills those sessions get.

In your terminal, Claude Code syncs those skills in sessions where you sign in with your claude.ai account. When the session starts, Claude Code downloads your account's skills into `~/.claude/skills/synced/` in the background, then checks claude.ai for changes while the session runs. When a check finds that a skill was added, edited, or turned off on claude.ai, Claude Code adds, updates, or removes it in the running session without a restart. Syncing in terminal sessions requires Claude Code v2.1.273 or later.

The checks run less often while the session is idle:

* **While you or Claude work in the session**: a check runs about every 10 minutes.
* **While the session is idle**: a check runs about every 40 minutes. When you type in the session again, Claude Code checks within a few minutes if the last check was more than 10 minutes ago.

The sync never delays startup, because Claude waits for a skill's download only when it invokes that skill. A short [non-interactive](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/headless.md) run can therefore finish before a newly added skill downloads, in which case a later session downloads it. To make a non-interactive run download your skills and wait for the list before it answers the prompt, set [`CLAUDE_CODE_SYNC_SKILLS`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/env-vars#variables) to `1`. Before v2.1.273, terminal sessions downloaded them only in a `-p` run with this variable set.

Claude Code syncs only in a session that signs in with your claude.ai account and [fetches feature flags from Anthropic](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/env-vars.md#features-that-need-feature-flag-fetching). It doesn't sync in these sessions:

* A session that doesn't use a sign-in stored by `/login`, such as one that authenticates with an API key, or one where `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, or an `apiKeyHelper` script supplies the credential
* A session that doesn't fetch feature flags, such as one on Amazon Bedrock or one where you set `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC`
* A session in [bare mode](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/headless.md#start-faster-with-bare-mode) or one you start with `--safe-mode`
* A session where your organization's managed settings [lock skills to plugin sources](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings-reference.md#strictpluginonlycustomization-skills), or one you start with a [`--setting-sources`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference#cli-flags) list that leaves out `user`

If you sign in with `/login` during a session, restart Claude Code to start syncing.

Skills that an earlier session synced stay on disk. Claude Code loads them in later sessions signed in to the same account, even when it can't reach claude.ai.

Claude Code downloads synced skills and never uploads them. If you or Claude edit a file under `~/.claude/skills/synced/`, the change isn't saved to your claude.ai account, and a later sync can overwrite or remove it. To change a synced skill, update it on claude.ai; the next sync downloads the new version.

To see which skills synced, run `/skills`. The menu lists them under `claude.ai sync`.

Some of Anthropic's skills, such as `pdf` and `xlsx`, always sync. For the rest, turn a skill on or off in your skills settings on claude.ai to change whether it syncs.

To stop syncing on a machine, set [`syncClaudeAiSkills`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings-reference#syncclaudeaiskills) to `false` in your user settings. Claude Code stops downloading, and the next time it starts it moves the skills it already synced to `~/.claude/skills/.trash/` and no longer loads them. Your organization can turn syncing off for everyone by turning off Skills on claude.ai. To stop syncing while leaving Skills on, it can set the same key in [managed settings](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/managed-settings.md).

If your organization turns Skills off on claude.ai, Claude Code removes the downloaded skills and they stop loading. The removed skills move to `~/.claude/skills/.trash/`, where you can recover the files until the [retention sweep](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-directory.md#cleaned-up-automatically) deletes them. Once your organization turns Skills back on, Claude Code downloads the skills you enabled at the next sync.

#### When a synced skill name matches another command

You can invoke a synced skill by its short name, `/<name>`, or by its full name, `/anthropic-skills:<name>`. When another command uses the short name, `/<name>` runs the other command, and the synced skill runs only as `/anthropic-skills:<name>`. With a local `deploy` skill and a synced `deploy`, `/deploy` runs the local skill and `/anthropic-skills:deploy` runs the synced one. Before v2.1.269, a synced skill had only its short name.

In the `/` menu, `/skills`, and `/context`, a synced skill appears under its short name, or under its full name while another command uses the short name. Run `/skills` in your session. A note under the list explains each synced skill that lost its short name. If one of your personal skills or command files in `~/.claude/` uses the name, the note also says what to rename or delete to free it.

From v2.1.269 through v2.1.280, these lists showed every synced skill under its full name, and `/skills` had no such note; both changed in v2.1.281.

The command that uses the short name can be any of these:

* A built-in command or a [bundled skill](#bundled-skills), including one that's unavailable in your session, for example after you turn bundled skills off
* A skill at any [local level](#where-skills-live) or a file in `.claude/commands/`
* A plugin skill
* An [MCP prompt](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp.md#use-mcp-prompts-as-commands)

Claude Code labels synced skills so you can tell where they came from. The `/skills` menu and `/context` group synced skills under `claude.ai sync`, and the `/` command menu marks them as coming from claude.ai.

When it compares names, Claude Code ignores case, spacing, and invisible characters, and treats compatibility forms such as fullwidth letters and dash variants as their plain equivalents. For example, a synced skill named `Commit` and a local skill named `commit` count as the same name, so `/commit` keeps running your local skill.

A name that differs only by a look-alike letter from another alphabet counts as a different name, and the `claude.ai sync` label is how you tell the two apart. These checks and labels require Claude Code v2.1.228 or later.

#### Names reserved for synced skills

Claude Code reserves the name `anthropic-skills`, and every name inside that namespace such as `anthropic-skills:pdf`, for skills synced from claude.ai, so a synced skill's full name never runs anything else. The name is reserved in every session, whether or not you sign in with a claude.ai account.

* **A skill folder, a frontmatter `name`, a file or subfolder in `.claude/commands/`, or a [saved workflow](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/workflows.md#save-the-workflow-for-reuse)**: it doesn't load. A [startup notice](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/errors.md#a-skill-command-or-workflow-wasnt-loaded-because-its-name-is-reserved) names the first item to rename or edit.
* **A plugin named `anthropic-skills`**: it loads. When one of its skills and a synced skill are both named `<name>`, `/anthropic-skills:<name>` runs the synced skill.
* **An MCP server named `anthropic-skills`**: it connects and its tools work, but [its prompts don't appear as commands](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp.md#use-mcp-prompts-as-commands). Rename the server in your MCP configuration to list them.

#### How Claude Code handles the frontmatter of a synced skill

Claude Code applies two rules to a synced skill's frontmatter:

* The frontmatter applies in every kind of session, so an `allowed-tools` grant goes through the normal [permission flow](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions.md). If your organization sets `allowManagedPermissionRulesOnly`, the grant [doesn't apply](#when-only-managed-permission-rules-apply).
* Claude Code sanitizes the display text the skill supplies, such as its description. It removes control characters, and in text that reaches Claude, such as the description, it also escapes angle brackets so the text can't imitate Claude Code's internal formatting. This sanitization requires Claude Code v2.1.228 or later.

#### How Claude Code handles the body of a synced skill

What Claude Code does with a synced skill's body depends on where the session runs:

* In a cloud session, the body keeps the behavior a local skill has, because the session runs in an isolated container.
* In a Cowork session on your desktop, the body keeps the behavior a local skill has, except that Claude Code replaces every `!` command line with the [`disableSkillShellExecution` placeholder](#inject-dynamic-context), as it does for every skill you supply there.
* In a terminal or VS Code session, Claude Code doesn't run [`!` commands](#inject-dynamic-context), doesn't attach the files that `@` references name, and doesn't substitute the `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}` and `${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}` placeholders. The `@` references and both placeholders reach Claude as literal text. A `!` command line reaches Claude as literal text too, or as the `disableSkillShellExecution` placeholder when that setting is on. This handling requires Claude Code v2.1.228 or later.

### Edit a skill during a session

Claude Code watches skill directories for file changes, except in [bare mode](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/headless.md#start-faster-with-bare-mode). When you add, edit, or remove a skill under `~/.claude/skills/`, the project `.claude/skills/`, or a `.claude/skills/` inside an `--add-dir` directory, Claude Code picks up the change within the current session, without a restart.

If you create a top-level skills directory that didn't exist when the session started, run [`/reload-skills`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/commands#all-commands) to pick up the skills you put there. Claude Code isn't watching that directory yet, so run `/reload-skills` again after each later change there.

> [!NOTE]
>
> Live change detection covers `SKILL.md` text. For a skill folder that is also a [plugin](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugins/loading.md#plugins-shared-through-a-repository), changes to `.mcp.json`, `agents/`, and `output-styles/` need `/reload-plugins` to take effect. Changes to `hooks/` need it too, except a [mod](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugins/mods/overview.md)'s module file when it [reloads on its own](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/env-vars.md).

### Remove a skill

How you remove a skill depends on where it came from:

* **Personal or project skill**: delete the skill's directory, `~/.claude/skills/<skill-name>/` or `.claude/skills/<skill-name>/`. Claude Code [drops it from `/skills` in the current session](#live-change-detection); content Claude Code already loaded from it follows the [skill content lifecycle](#skill-content-lifecycle).
* **Enterprise skill**: an administrator deletes the skill's directory from `.claude/skills/` inside the [managed settings directory](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/managed-settings.md#delivery-mechanisms), for example `/etc/claude-code/.claude/skills/<skill-name>/` on Linux.
* **Plugin skill**: disable or uninstall the plugin that provides it, from the `/plugin` menu or with `/plugin uninstall <plugin-name>@<marketplace-name>`. Claude Code unloads the plu

## Decisions and assumptions
- **Usage dedup (confirmed):** Claude Code writes one `assistant` record per content block; all records of one API message repeat the same `message.id` and an identical `message.usage` (7234187e main: 21 records / 9 ids, 0 ids with differing usage; worker 6/5; repo logs 10/5 and 12/6). **Rule: count usage once per `message.id` within a transcript; records without an id are each counted.** Phase 03's independent sum must use the same rule. With this rule the totals equal cost-state `modelUsage` (see above).
- cost-state in the main transcript covers the whole session including workers. `Transcript.cost_usd` = last `cost-state.totalCostUSD` in that transcript (so main only; workers None). Phase 02 should show the session cost from `main.cost_usd` and not add worker costs.
- Token mapping: `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens` -> `cache_read_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens` -> `cache_creation_tokens`.
- Skipped counting: malformed lines, non-object lines, unknown record types, user/assistant records without usable content, unknown assistant/user block shapes, and a cost-state without a numeric totalCostUSD. Known internal types (`INTERNAL_RECORD_TYPES` + prefix `file-history-`, from the plan's list plus `api-request-shape`) are ignored and NOT counted, so the skipped count signals real format drift instead of being always large.
- `errors` counts tool results with `is_error` or `is_denied` (real denials carry both).
- Steps: `index` is 1-based within a transcript; `anchor` = `step-<transcript name>-<index>` (unique across a task since session ids and agent ids are unique). Tool call `text` is `json.dumps(input, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)`. Tool result `text` flattens list content: text blocks joined by newline, other blocks as `[<type> block]`. Tool results also get `tool_name` copied from their call. Non-text user blocks (e.g. image) become a `user` step `[<type> block]`. `redacted_thinking` is treated as `thinking`. Thinking text is empty in current transcripts (redacted), kept as an empty `thinking` step.
- System step text: `content` if a non-empty string, else the `subtype`; non-empty `hookErrors` are appended as JSON.
- Timestamps: `first_timestamp`/`last_timestamp` are min/max of step timestamps (ISO strings compare correctly).
- Worker linking searches tool calls in main and all workers of the session (covers nested workers), by `.meta.json` `toolUseId`, regardless of tool name. Missing/malformed meta -> worker unlinked, `actor` falls back to agent_type then `agent-<id>`.
- Ordering: sessions by main first timestamp (no timestamp last, then by id); workers likewise by first timestamp. Documents: plan.md, status.md, summary.md, then sorted phases/*.md, then sorted progress/*.md.
- A missing main `<session-id>.jsonl` gives an empty main Transcript (still lists workers).

## Interface deviations from the brief
- None in names/fields. Additions: `load_task` raises `FileNotFoundError` if the task directory does not exist (phase 02's main() should catch it and exit non-zero). Helper `TimelineBuilder` class and module constants `INTERNAL_RECORD_TYPES`, `TASK_DOCUMENT_NAMES`, `TASK_DOCUMENT_FOLDERS` exist but are internal.

#7 · 2026-11-17T15:29:37.998Z · tool result · Bash
# Plan: Inspect-style `report.html` for a poe task ## Objectiveshow the rest (11,354 more characters)
Turn what poe saves for a task in `poe/<task-slug>/` (plan, status, phase briefs, progress notes, summary, and the copied Claude Code transcripts in `logs/`) into one self-contained `poe/<task-slug>/report.html`. The page, styled after the Inspect AI log viewer, lets a person follow, step by step, what the orchestrator and each worker did: prompts, replies, tool calls and their results, errors, and token and cost totals.

The page has two later uses, which shape it now but are not built here:

- **Stacktrace:** when a run goes wrong, errored and denied tool calls can be found quickly and traced back to the step that caused them.
- **LLM-as-judge input:** all content is plain, server-rendered HTML text (no data hidden in JavaScript), so a model can be given the file as is.

## Scope

In scope:

- `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py` (new): a Python 3 script using only the standard library. `python3 poe_report.py poe/<task-slug>` reads that task directory and writes `poe/<task-slug>/report.html`.
- `tests/test_poe_report.py` and `tests/fixtures/` (new): `unittest` tests against a small, hand-written transcript fixture.
- `skills/poe/SKILL.md`:
  - a new `report [plan-path]` mode that (re)builds the report;
  - an automatic report build at the end of `execute` and `resume`, right after the log copy;
  - `report.html` added to the Files layout.
- `README.md`: the `report` mode, `report.html` in the layout, how to run the script by hand, and gitignore advice.

Out of scope:

- The LLM-as-judge itself, scoring, and any JSON or other machine-readable export.
- Converting to Inspect AI's `.eval` log format, or depending on `inspect-ai` or any other pip package. "Inspect style" means the look and layout, not the tool.
- A live server, auto-refresh, search boxes, charts, or comparing several tasks.
- Changing how logs are copied. The report works with whatever is in `logs/`.
- Rendering Markdown to HTML. The task's `.md` files are shown as preformatted text.
- The older draft at `plans/poe-report-dashboard/plan.md` (written before output moved to `poe/`). This file replaces it; it is left untouched for the user to delete.

## Assumptions

- The layout is the one in the current `SKILL.md`: `poe/<slug>/{plan.md, status.md, summary.md, phases/*.md, progress/*.md, logs/<session-id>/<session-id>.jsonl, logs/<session-id>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl, agent-<id>.meta.json}`. Any of these may be missing, and there may be several sessions (one per `plan`, `split`, `execute`, `resume` run).
- The Claude Code transcript format is not documented and changes between versions. Checked on this machine (Claude Code 2.1.326) against existing transcripts:
  - Each line is a JSON record with a `type`. The records that matter are `user` and `assistant` (with `message.content` blocks `text`, `thinking`, `tool_use`, `tool_result`), `system`, and `cost-state` (`totalCostUSD`, `totalDuration`, `modelUsage`). Other types (`attachment`, `api-request`, `api-request-blob`, `mode`, `session-owner`, `atis-latch`, `last-prompt`, `ai-title`, `permission-mode`, `tools-used`, `file-history-*`, `queue-operation`, ...) are internal and skipped.
  - `assistant` records carry `timestamp`, `message.model`, and `message.usage` (input, output, cache read and cache creation tokens).
  - `user` records holding a `tool_result` block may carry `toolUseResult` and `permissionDecision` (`decision`, `source`). The block itself may have `is_error: true`.
  - A worker's `agent-<id>.meta.json` holds `agentType`, `description` and `toolUseId`. `toolUseId` matches the `id` of the `Agent` `tool_use` block in the main transcript, which is how a worker is linked to the step that started it.
- A tool result counts as an error when `is_error` is true, or as denied when `permissionDecision.decision` is present and not `accept`. Both are read defensively.
- Records are shown in file order (the order Claude Code appended them). The `parentUuid` tree is not reconstructed.
- `python3` (3.9 or later) is available wherever poe runs. This machine has 3.12.
- Skills substitute `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}`, so `SKILL.md` can call the bundled script by its path. **To be confirmed during implementation** against the skills documentation; otherwise use `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`.

## Approach

### 1. The script, `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py`

One file of small functions, in three steps.

**Load.** Read the task's `.md` files as text. For each `logs/<session-id>/` folder, parse the main `.jsonl` and each `subagents/agent-*.jsonl` with its `.meta.json`. A line that is not valid JSON, or a record without the expected fields, is counted and skipped; it never stops the script.

**Build a simple timeline per transcript.** Turn the useful records into a flat list of steps, each with: time, actor (orchestrator or worker name), kind, and text. The kinds are:

- user prompt
- assistant text
- thinking (collapsed)
- tool call (tool name and its input)
- tool result (output; marked as an error or denial as above)
- system or hook message

Each tool call is paired with its result by `tool_use_id`. An `Agent` tool call links to the worker's own timeline. Per transcript, and in total, the script adds up tokens, the number of tool calls and errors, the model or models, the duration (first to last timestamp), and the cost from the last `cost-state` record when present.

**Render.** Write one HTML file with inline CSS and no external resources, so it opens offline and can be attached or emailed as a single file. The layout follows Inspect's viewer:

- **Sidebar:** anchor links to the overview, the errors list, the task documents, and each session, with its workers indented under it.
- **Overview cards:** the task slug, the date generated, the status table from `status.md` as written, the number of sessions and workers, tool calls, errors, tokens, cost, duration, and the number of skipped records.
- **Errors:** every errored or denied tool call across all transcripts, each with its tool name, a one-line excerpt, and a link to the step. This is the stacktrace view.
- **Task documents:** `plan.md`, `status.md`, `summary.md`, phase briefs and progress notes, each in a collapsible `<details>` block of preformatted text.
- **Session timelines:** one section per session, ordered by first timestamp, then one per worker. Each step is a row with a coloured left border by kind, a timestamp, and its content. Tool inputs and outputs are in monospace inside `<details>`, with the first few lines visible. Error rows are red. A worker section starts with its type, description, model and totals, and links back to the step that started it.
- Light and dark colours through `prefers-color-scheme`. `<details>` handles collapsing, so the page needs no JavaScript.

All text from the logs goes through `html.escape`, because transcripts contain arbitrary file contents and command output. Content is never truncated in the HTML. Long blocks are collapsed, not cut, so the file stays complete for a stacktrace or a judge.

If `logs/` is missing or empty, the report still renders the documents and says that no transcripts were found.

The script prints the path of the written report and exits 0. It exits non-zero only if the task directory does not exist or the report cannot be written.

### 2. Skill changes, `skills/poe/SKILL.md`

- Operating modes gain `report [plan-path]`: copy the logs (as in the Logs section), then run `python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/poe_report.py" poe/<slug>` and give the user the path of the report.
- Execute mode step 9 and `resume`: after writing `summary.md` and copying the logs, build the report the same way. A failure is reported in the reply but never blocks the task, the same rule as for log copies.
- Files layout: add `report.html   step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/`.

### 3. README

Add the `report` mode to the usage list, `report.html` to the layout, the manual command `python3 <plugin>/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py poe/<slug>`, and a note that `report.html` contains the full transcripts, so it belongs in `.gitignore` alongside `logs/`.

## Acceptance criteria

- `python3 -m unittest discover tests` passes. The tests cover:
  - parsing the fixture: the expected number of steps, tool calls paired with their results, an error flagged, and a worker linked by `toolUseId`;
  - skipping unknown record types and malformed lines without crashing;
  - HTML escaping: a `<script>` string in a tool result appears escaped in the output;
  - a task directory without `logs/` still produces a report.
- Running the script on real transcripts (a poe session from this project, including one with workers, copied into a scratch `poe/<slug>/logs/`) writes a `report.html` that opens in a browser and shows:
  - all sessions and their workers;
  - every tool call, in order, with its result;
  - every `is_error` tool result, listed in the Errors section;
  - token totals that match a separate sum over `message.usage` in the same files.
- The report opens with no network access (no external URLs in the file).
- `SKILL.md` documents the `report` mode and the automatic build. `README.md` matches it. `claude plugin validate .` still passes.
- `/poe:poe report <slug>` run with `claude --plugin-dir .` in a scratch directory produces `poe/<slug>/report.html`.

## Risks

- **Transcript format changes.** The parser relies on undocumented fields. Mitigation: read fields defensively, skip what is unknown, and show the count of skipped records in the overview, so silent loss is visible.
- **Missing logs.** A log copy can fail (for example, refused under a restrictive permission mode). Mitigation: the report degrades to documents only and says why.
- **Permission prompts.** Running `python3` on a script outside the working directory may need approval in restrictive modes. Mitigation: the same "report and carry on" rule as for logs. The README gives the manual command.
- **Sensitive data.** The report contains everything in the transcripts, including file contents and command output. Mitigation: gitignore advice. Nothing is uploaded.
- **Large files.** A long execute run can produce several MB of transcripts, and so a large HTML file. Acceptable for a local file. Collapsed `<details>` keep it usable in a browser.

## Testing and validation

1. Unit tests: `python3 -m unittest discover tests`, using a small, hand-written fixture in `tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/` with a main transcript, one worker transcript and meta file, one errored tool call, one unknown record type, and one malformed line. The fixture holds no real session data.
2. Real data: copy the transcripts of a real poe session from `~/.claude/projects/` into a scratch `poe/<slug>/logs/` (outside the repository, never committed), run the script, and check the report in a browser against the acceptance criteria above. Check the token totals with a short independent `python3` sum over the same files.
3. End to end: `claude --plugin-dir .` in a scratch directory, run `/poe:poe report <slug>` on that scratch task, and confirm that `report.html` is written and the skill reports its path.
4. Static checks: `grep -nE '(src|href)="https?:' report.html` finds no external resources (escaped URLs inside log text don't match), and `claude plugin validate .` passes.

#9 · 2026-11-17T15:29:40.819Z · tool result · Bash
#!/usr/bin/env python3 """Build a step-by-step report for a poe task directory. show the rest (14,441 more characters)
This file currently holds the Load and Timeline parts: it reads a task's
documents and Claude Code transcripts from poe/<task-slug>/ and turns them
into plain dataclasses. Python 3 standard library only.

The transcript format is undocumented, so every field is read defensively.
Lines that are not valid JSON, records of unknown type and records without
the expected fields are skipped and counted, never fatal.
"""

import json
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import List, Optional, Set, Tuple

# Record types Claude Code writes for its own bookkeeping. They are ignored
# without being counted as skipped; any other unknown type is counted.
INTERNAL_RECORD_TYPES = {
    "attachment",
    "api-request",
    "api-request-blob",
    "api-request-shape",
    "mode",
    "session-owner",
    "atis-latch",
    "last-prompt",
    "ai-title",
    "permission-mode",
    "tools-used",
    "queue-operation",
}
INTERNAL_RECORD_PREFIXES = ("file-history-",)

TASK_DOCUMENT_NAMES = ["plan.md", "status.md", "summary.md"]
TASK_DOCUMENT_FOLDERS = ["phases", "progress"]


@dataclass
class Step:
    index: int
    timestamp: Optional[str]
    kind: str  # user, assistant, thinking, tool_call, tool_result, system
    text: str
    anchor: str
    tool_name: Optional[str] = None
    tool_use_id: Optional[str] = None
    is_error: bool = False
    is_denied: bool = False
    result: Optional["Step"] = None
    worker: Optional["Transcript"] = None


@dataclass
class Transcript:
    name: str
    actor: str
    agent_type: Optional[str] = None
    description: Optional[str] = None
    steps: List[Step] = field(default_factory=list)
    models: Set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
    input_tokens: int = 0
    output_tokens: int = 0
    cache_read_tokens: int = 0
    cache_creation_tokens: int = 0
    tool_calls: int = 0
    errors: int = 0
    first_timestamp: Optional[str] = None
    last_timestamp: Optional[str] = None
    cost_usd: Optional[float] = None
    skipped: int = 0
    parent_step: Optional[Step] = None


@dataclass
class Session:
    session_id: str
    main: Transcript
    workers: List[Transcript] = field(default_factory=list)


@dataclass
class Task:
    slug: str
    task_dir: Path
    documents: List[Tuple[str, str]] = field(default_factory=list)
    sessions: List[Session] = field(default_factory=list)
    skipped: int = 0


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- documents


def read_text(path: Path) -> str:
    return path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")


def load_documents(task_dir: Path) -> List[Tuple[str, str]]:
    documents = []
    for name in TASK_DOCUMENT_NAMES:
        path = task_dir / name
        if path.is_file():
            documents.append((name, read_text(path)))
    for folder in TASK_DOCUMENT_FOLDERS:
        for path in sorted((task_dir / folder).glob("*.md")):
            documents.append((f"{folder}/{path.name}", read_text(path)))
    return documents


# -------------------------------------------------------------- transcripts


def read_json_lines(path: Path) -> Tuple[List[dict], int]:
    """Return the JSON object records of a .jsonl file and the count of bad lines."""
    records = []
    bad_lines = 0
    if not path.is_file():
        return records, bad_lines
    with path.open(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as handle:
        for line in handle:
            if not line.strip():
                continue
            try:
                record = json.loads(line)
            except json.JSONDecodeError:
                bad_lines += 1
                continue
            if isinstance(record, dict):
                records.append(record)
            else:
                bad_lines += 1
    return records, bad_lines


def is_internal_record_type(record_type) -> bool:
    if not isinstance(record_type, str):
        return False
    return record_type in INTERNAL_RECORD_TYPES or record_type.startswith(INTERNAL_RECORD_PREFIXES)


def tool_result_text(content) -> str:
    """Flatten a tool_result's content, which is a string or a list of blocks."""
    if isinstance(content, str):
        return content
    if not isinstance(content, list):
        return ""
    parts = []
    for block in content:
        if isinstance(block, dict) and block.get("type") == "text":
            parts.append(str(block.get("text", "")))
        elif isinstance(block, dict):
            parts.append(f"[{block.get('type', 'unknown')} block]")
        else:
            parts.append(str(block))
    return "\n".join(parts)


def is_denied_record(record: dict) -> bool:
    decision = record.get("permissionDecision")
    if not isinstance(decision, dict) or "decision" not in decision:
        return False
    return decision.get("decision") != "accept"


def system_text(record: dict) -> str:
    content = record.get("content")
    text = content if isinstance(content, str) and content else str(record.get("subtype") or "system")
    hook_errors = record.get("hookErrors")
    if hook_errors:
        text += "\nhook errors: " + json.dumps(hook_errors, indent=2)
    return text


def add_usage(transcript: Transcript, usage) -> None:
    if not isinstance(usage, dict):
        return
    transcript.input_tokens += int(usage.get("input_tokens") or 0)
    transcript.output_tokens += int(usage.get("output_tokens") or 0)
    transcript.cache_read_tokens += int(usage.get("cache_read_input_tokens") or 0)
    transcript.cache_creation_tokens += int(usage.get("cache_creation_input_tokens") or 0)


class TimelineBuilder:
    """Turns one transcript's records into Steps and running totals."""

    def __init__(self, transcript: Transcript):
        self.transcript = transcript
        self.calls_by_id = {}
        # Claude Code writes one assistant record per content block, each
        # repeating message.id and message.usage, so usage is counted once per id.
        self.counted_message_ids = set()

    def add_step(self, record: dict, kind: str, text: str, **fields) -> Step:
        transcript = self.transcript
        index = len(transcript.steps) + 1
        timestamp = record.get("timestamp")
        if not isinstance(timestamp, str):
            timestamp = None
        step = Step(
            index=index,
            timestamp=timestamp,
            kind=kind,
            text=text,
            anchor=f"step-{transcript.name}-{index}",
            **fields,
        )
        transcript.steps.append(step)
        if timestamp:
            if transcript.first_timestamp is None or timestamp < transcript.first_timestamp:
                transcript.first_timestamp = timestamp
            if transcript.last_timestamp is None or timestamp > transcript.last_timestamp:
                transcript.last_timestamp = timestamp
        return step

    def add_record(self, record: dict) -> None:
        record_type = record.get("type")
        if record_type == "user":
            self.add_user_record(record)
        elif record_type == "assistant":
            self.add_assistant_record(record)
        elif record_type == "system":
            self.add_step(record, "system", system_text(record))
        elif record_type == "cost-state":
            cost = record.get("totalCostUSD")
            if isinstance(cost, (int, float)):
                self.transcript.cost_usd = float(cost)
            else:
                self.transcript.skipped += 1
        elif not is_internal_record_type(record_type):
            self.transcript.skipped += 1

    def add_user_record(self, record: dict) -> None:
        message = record.get("message")
        content = message.get("content") if isinstance(message, dict) else None
        if isinstance(content, str):
            self.add_step(record, "user", content)
            return
        if not isinstance(content, list):
            self.transcript.skipped += 1
            return
        for block in content:
            block_type = block.get("type") if isinstance(block, dict) else None
            if block_type == "tool_result":
                self.add_tool_result(record, block)
            elif block_type == "text":
                self.add_step(record, "user", str(block.get("text", "")))
            elif block_type is not None:
                self.add_step(record, "user", f"[{block_type} block]")
            else:
                self.transcript.skipped += 1

    def add_tool_result(self, record: dict, block: dict) -> None:
        tool_use_id = block.get("tool_use_id")
        call = self.calls_by_id.get(tool_use_id)
        step = self.add_step(
            record,
            "tool_result",
            tool_result_text(block.get("content")),
            tool_name=call.tool_name if call else None,
            tool_use_id=tool_use_id,
            is_error=block.get("is_error") is True,
            is_denied=is_denied_record(record),
        )
        if call is not None:
            call.result = step
        if step.is_error or step.is_denied:
            self.transcript.errors += 1

    def add_assistant_record(self, record: dict) -> None:
        message = record.get("message")
        if not isinstance(message, dict) or not isinstance(message.get("content"), list):
            self.transcript.skipped += 1
            return
        model = message.get("model")
        if isinstance(model, str) and model:
            self.transcript.models.add(model)
        message_id = message.get("id")
        if message_id is None or message_id not in self.counted_message_ids:
            add_usage(self.transcript, message.get("usage"))
            if message_id is not None:
                self.counted_message_ids.add(message_id)
        for block in message["content"]:
            self.add_assistant_block(record, block)

    def add_assistant_block(self, record: dict, block) -> None:
        block_type = block.get("type") if isinstance(block, dict) else None
        if block_type == "text":
            self.add_step(record, "assistant", str(block.get("text", "")))
        elif block_type in ("thinking", "redacted_thinking"):
            self.add_step(record, "thinking", str(block.get("thinking") or ""))
        elif block_type == "tool_use":
            tool_use_id = block.get("id")
            step = self.add_step(
                record,
                "tool_call",
                json.dumps(block.get("input"), indent=2, ensure_ascii=False),
                tool_name=block.get("name"),
                tool_use_id=tool_use_id,
            )
            self.transcript.tool_calls += 1
            if tool_use_id is not None:
                self.calls_by_id[tool_use_id] = step
        else:
            self.transcript.skipped += 1


def load_transcript(path: Path, name: str, actor: str) -> Transcript:
    transcript = Transcript(name=name, actor=actor)
    records, bad_lines = read_json_lines(path)
    transcript.skipped += bad_lines
    builder = TimelineBuilder(transcript)
    for record in records:
        builder.add_record(record)
    return transcript


def read_meta(path: Path) -> dict:
    try:
        meta = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    except (OSError, ValueError):
        return {}
    return meta if isinstance(meta, dict) else {}


def load_worker(transcript_path: Path) -> Tuple[Transcript, Optional[str]]:
    """Load a worker transcript; also return the toolUseId from its .meta.json."""
    name = transcript_path.stem  # agent-<id>
    meta = read_meta(transcript_path.with_name(name + ".meta.json"))
    agent_type = meta.get("agentType")
    description = meta.get("description")
    actor = description or agent_type or name
    worker = load_transcript(transcript_path, name, actor)
    worker.agent_type = agent_type
    worker.description = description
    return worker, meta.get("toolUseId")


def sort_key_by_time(transcript: Transcript):
    # Transcripts without timestamps go last.
    return (transcript.first_timestamp is None, transcript.first_timestamp or "", transcript.name)


def load_session(session_dir: Path) -> Session:
    session_id = session_dir.name
    main = load_transcript(session_dir / f"{session_id}.jsonl", session_id, "orchestrator")

    workers = []
    worker_tool_use_ids = []
    for path in sorted((session_dir / "subagents").glob("agent-*.jsonl")):
        worker, tool_use_id = load_worker(path)
        workers.append(worker)
        worker_tool_use_ids.append(tool_use_id)

    # Link each worker to the call that started it, whatever the tool's name
    # (Agent, or Task in older versions). Search every transcript in case a
    # worker started its own workers.
    calls_by_id = {}
    for transcript in [main] + workers:
        for step in transcript.steps:
            if step.kind == "tool_call" and step.tool_use_id:
                calls_by_id[step.tool_use_id] = step
    for worker, tool_use_id in zip(workers, worker_tool_use_ids):
        call = calls_by_id.get(tool_use_id)
        if call is not None:
            call.worker = worker
            worker.parent_step = call

    workers.sort(key=sort_key_by_time)
    return Session(session_id=session_id, main=main, workers=workers)


def load_task(task_dir: Path) -> Task:
    task_dir = Path(task_dir)
    if not task_dir.is_dir():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"task directory not found: {task_dir}")
    task = Task(slug=task_dir.name, task_dir=task_dir, documents=load_documents(task_dir))

    logs_dir = task_dir / "logs"
    if logs_dir.is_dir():
        for session_dir in sorted(path for path in logs_dir.iterdir() if path.is_dir()):
            task.sessions.append(load_session(session_dir))
    task.sessions.sort(key=lambda session: sort_key_by_time(session.main))

    for session in task.sessions:
        task.skipped += session.main.skipped + sum(worker.skipped for worker in session.workers)
    return task

#11 · 2026-11-17T15:29:43.462Z · tool result · Bash
import sys import tempfile import unittestshow the rest (7,824 more characters)
from pathlib import Path

REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO_ROOT / "skills" / "poe" / "scripts"))

import poe_report  # noqa: E402

FIXTURE_TASK = REPO_ROOT / "tests" / "fixtures" / "poe" / "sample-task"
SESSION_ID = "11111111-2222-3333-4444-555555555555"


def find_call(transcript, tool_use_id):
    for step in transcript.steps:
        if step.kind == "tool_call" and step.tool_use_id == tool_use_id:
            return step
    raise AssertionError(f"no tool call {tool_use_id}")


class LoadFixtureTaskTest(unittest.TestCase):
    @classmethod
    def setUpClass(cls):
        cls.task = poe_report.load_task(FIXTURE_TASK)
        cls.session = cls.task.sessions[0]
        cls.main = cls.session.main
        cls.worker = cls.session.workers[0]

    def test_task_documents_in_order(self):
        paths = [path for path, _ in self.task.documents]
        self.assertEqual(paths, ["plan.md", "status.md", "phases/01-x.md", "progress/01a-x.md"])
        self.assertEqual(self.task.slug, "sample-task")

    def test_one_session_with_one_worker(self):
        self.assertEqual(len(self.task.sessions), 1)
        self.assertEqual(self.session.session_id, SESSION_ID)
        self.assertEqual(self.main.name, SESSION_ID)
        self.assertEqual(self.main.actor, "orchestrator")
        self.assertEqual(len(self.session.workers), 1)

    def test_step_counts_and_kinds(self):
        self.assertEqual(len(self.main.steps), 13)
        self.assertEqual(len(self.worker.steps), 4)
        kinds = [step.kind for step in self.main.steps]
        self.assertEqual(
            kinds,
            ["user", "thinking", "assistant"]
            + ["tool_call", "tool_result"] * 4
            + ["system", "assistant"],
        )

    def test_anchors_are_unique(self):
        anchors = [step.anchor for step in self.main.steps + self.worker.steps]
        self.assertEqual(len(anchors), len(set(anchors)))

    def test_each_tool_call_paired_with_its_result(self):
        for transcript in (self.main, self.worker):
            calls = [step for step in transcript.steps if step.kind == "tool_call"]
            self.assertEqual(len(calls), transcript.tool_calls)
            for call in calls:
                self.assertIsNotNone(call.result, call.tool_use_id)
                self.assertEqual(call.result.kind, "tool_result")
                self.assertEqual(call.result.tool_use_id, call.tool_use_id)
                self.assertEqual(call.result.tool_name, call.tool_name)
        self.assertEqual(self.main.tool_calls, 4)
        self.assertEqual(self.worker.tool_calls, 1)

    def test_error_flagged(self):
        result = find_call(self.main, "toolu_error_1").result
        self.assertTrue(result.is_error)
        self.assertFalse(result.is_denied)

    def test_denial_flagged(self):
        result = find_call(self.main, "toolu_denied_1").result
        self.assertTrue(result.is_denied)

    def test_accepted_results_not_flagged(self):
        for tool_use_id in ("toolu_agent_1", "toolu_read_1"):
            result = find_call(self.main, tool_use_id).result
            self.assertFalse(result.is_error)
            self.assertFalse(result.is_denied)
        self.assertEqual(self.main.errors, 2)
        self.assertEqual(self.worker.errors, 0)

    def test_text_kept_whole(self):
        result = find_call(self.main, "toolu_read_1").result
        self.assertEqual(result.text, "<script>alert(1)</script>")
        agent_result = find_call(self.main, "toolu_agent_1").result
        self.assertEqual(agent_result.text, "Worker done: sample.txt written.")
        self.assertEqual(self.main.steps[-2].text, "Stop hook ran.")

    def test_worker_linked_by_tool_use_id(self):
        call = find_call(self.main, "toolu_agent_1")
        self.assertIs(call.worker, self.worker)
        self.assertIs(self.worker.parent_step, call)
        self.assertEqual(self.worker.name, "agent-a0000000000000001")
        self.assertEqual(self.worker.agent_type, "poe:phase-worker")
        self.assertEqual(self.worker.description, "Execute unit 01a sample")
        self.assertEqual(self.worker.actor, "Execute unit 01a sample")

    def test_unknown_type_and_malformed_line_skipped_and_counted(self):
        # mystery-record and the malformed line; internal types are not counted.
        self.assertEqual(self.main.skipped, 2)
        self.assertEqual(self.worker.skipped, 0)
        self.assertEqual(self.task.skipped, 2)

    def test_token_totals_count_each_message_id_once(self):
        # msg_main_1 appears in two records but is counted once: 6 messages.
        self.assertEqual(self.main.input_tokens, 60)
        self.assertEqual(self.main.output_tokens, 120)
        self.assertEqual(self.main.cache_read_tokens, 600)
        self.assertEqual(self.main.cache_creation_tokens, 300)
        self.assertEqual(self.worker.input_tokens, 20)
        self.assertEqual(self.worker.output_tokens, 40)
        self.assertEqual(self.worker.cache_read_tokens, 200)
        self.assertEqual(self.worker.cache_creation_tokens, 100)

    def test_models_timestamps_and_cost(self):
        self.assertEqual(self.main.models, {"fake-model-1"})
        self.assertEqual(self.worker.models, {"fake-model-2"})
        self.assertEqual(self.main.first_timestamp, "2026-01-01T10:00:00.000Z")
        self.assertEqual(self.main.last_timestamp, "2026-01-01T10:00:29.000Z")
        self.assertAlmostEqual(self.main.cost_usd, 0.1234)
        self.assertIsNone(self.worker.cost_usd)


class LoadTaskWithoutLogsTest(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_task_without_logs_has_no_sessions(self):
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp:
            task_dir = Path(temp) / "no-logs"
            task_dir.mkdir()
            (task_dir / "plan.md").write_text("# Plan\n", encoding="utf-8")
            task = poe_report.load_task(task_dir)
        self.assertEqual(task.sessions, [])
        self.assertEqual(task.skipped, 0)
        self.assertEqual(task.documents, [("plan.md", "# Plan\n")])

    def test_missing_task_dir_raises(self):
        with self.assertRaises(FileNotFoundError):
            poe_report.load_task(FIXTURE_TASK / "does-not-exist")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

## Decisions and assumptions
- **Usage dedup (confirmed):** Claude Code writes one `assistant` record per content block; all records of one API message repeat the same `message.id` and an identical `message.usage` (7234187e main: 21 records / 9 ids, 0 ids with differing usage; worker 6/5; repo logs 10/5 and 12/6). **Rule: count usage once per `message.id` within a transcript; records without an id are each counted.** Phase 03's independent sum must use the same rule. With this rule the totals equal cost-state `modelUsage` (see above).
- cost-state in the main transcript covers the whole session including workers. `Transcript.cost_usd` = last `cost-state.totalCostUSD` in that transcript (so main only; workers None). Phase 02 should show the session cost from `main.cost_usd` and not add worker costs.
- Token mapping: `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens` -> `cache_read_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens` -> `cache_creation_tokens`.
- Skipped counting: malformed lines, non-object lines, unknown record types, user/assistant records without usable content, unknown assistant/user block shapes, and a cost-state without a numeric totalCostUSD. Known internal types (`INTERNAL_RECORD_TYPES` + prefix `file-history-`, from the plan's list plus `api-request-shape`) are ignored and NOT counted, so the skipped count signals real format drift instead of being always large.
- `errors` counts tool results with `is_error` or `is_denied` (real denials carry both).
- Steps: `index` is 1-based within a transcript; `anchor` = `step-<transcript name>-<index>` (unique across a task since session ids and agent ids are unique). Tool call `text` is `json.dumps(input, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)`. Tool result `text` flattens list content: text blocks joined by newline, other blocks as `[<type> block]`. Tool results also get `tool_name` copied from their call. Non-text user blocks (e.g. image) become a `user` step `[<type> block]`. `redacted_thinking` is treated as `thinking`. Thinking text is empty in current transcripts (redacted), kept as an empty `thinking` step.
- System step text: `content` if a non-empty string, else the `subtype`; non-empty `hookErrors` are appended as JSON.
- Timestamps: `first_timestamp`/`last_timestamp` are min/max of step timestamps (ISO strings compare correctly).
- Worker linking searches tool calls in main and all workers of the session (covers nested workers), by `.meta.json` `toolUseId`, regardless of tool name. Missing/malformed meta -> worker unlinked, `actor` falls back to agent_type then `agent-<id>`.
- Ordering: sessions by main first timestamp (no timestamp last, then by id); workers likewise by first timestamp. Documents: plan.md, status.md, summary.md, then sorted phases/*.md, then sorted progress/*.md.
- A missing main `<session-id>.jsonl` gives an empty main Transcript (still lists workers).

## Interface deviations from the brief
- None in names/fields. Additions: `load_task` raises `FileNotFoundError` if the task directory does not exist (phase 02's main() should catch it and exit non-zero). Helper `TimelineBuilder` class and module constants `INTERNAL_RECORD_TYPES`, `TASK_DOCUMENT_NAMES`, `TASK_DOCUMENT_FOLDERS` exist but are internal.

## Next step / remaining work
- None for 01a. Phase 02 adds rendering and `main()` on top of `load_task`.

## Decisions and assumptions
- Section ids: `overview`, `errors`, `documents`, `transcript-<name>` for each session main (name = session id) and worker (name = agent-<id>); steps use `Step.anchor`. With no sessions, a `sessions` section says "No transcripts were found in logs/.".
- Cost: sum of `main.cost_usd` over sessions; worker costs not added; "n/a" when none.
- Duration: per session, earliest to latest timestamp across main and workers; overview duration = sum of session durations (sessions are separate runs, possibly days apart, so first-to-last over the task would mislead).
- Tokens: overview shows the total of the four counts plus a wide card with the breakdown; sessions show session (main+workers) and orchestrator-only totals; workers show their own.
- Collapsing: `collapsible_text` shows text whole in `<pre>` when short; otherwise `<details>` whose summary shows the first N lines (3 for tool input/output, 12 for user/assistant/system text), capped at N*200 characters so a single huge line also collapses; the body holds exactly the rest, so preview + rest = whole text (tested). Preview uses `<span class="mono preview">` because `<pre>` is not allowed inside `<summary>`.
- Thinking: always collapsed; empty thinking (redacted in current transcripts) shows "(thinking text not recorded)".
- Errors list: one item per errored/denied tool result: link "<actor> · step N" to the step anchor, badge (denied wins over error since real denials have both flags), tool name, first non-empty line of the result (CSS ellipsis; the full text is in the step).
- Tool calls link to their result ("result #N"); the Agent call links to the worker section; the worker header links back ("started by: step #N (Agent)"), or says "not found" when unlinked.
- Generated date: local time, `%Y-%m-%d %H:%M %Z`; `render_report` takes `generated` so tests are deterministic.
- `main` catches OSError on load (FileNotFoundError included) -> "could not read the task", and OSError on write -> "could not write the report"; both exit 1, message on stderr. Success prints the path (as given + /report.html) on stdout.
- Added `<meta name="color-scheme" content="light dark">` so form controls/scrollbars follow the theme.

106-## Risks
107-
108-- **Transcript format changes.** The parser relies on undocumented fields. Mitigation: read fields defensively, skip what is unknown, and show the count of skipped records in the overview, so silent loss is visible.
109-- **Missing logs.** A log copy can fail (for example, refused under a restrictive permission mode). Mitigation: the report degrades to documents only and says why.
110-- **Permission prompts.** Running `python3` on a script outside the working directory may need approval in restrictive modes. Mitigation: the same "report and carry on" rule as for logs. The README gives the manual command.
111-- **Sensitive data.** The report contains everything in the transcripts, including file contents and command output. Mitigation: gitignore advice. Nothing is uploaded.
112-- **Large files.** A long execute run can produce several MB of transcripts, and so a large HTML file. Acceptable for a local file. Collapsed `<details>` keep it usable in a browser.
113-
114-## Testing and validation
115-
116-1. Unit tests: `python3 -m unittest discover tests`, using a small, hand-written fixture in `tests/fixtures/poe/sample-task/` with a main transcript, one worker transcript and meta file, one errored tool call, one unknown record type, and one malformed line. The fixture holds no real session data.
